\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\big(s\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z)+s^{\prime}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z^{\prime})\big)^{\alpha/2}-\big(s\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z)\big)^{\alpha/2}-\big(s^{\prime}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z^{\prime})\big)^{\alpha/2}\Big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

Before giving the proof of Proposition
3.11
, let us introduce the parameter
\sigma_{N}\coloneqq\sqrt{N{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{x}_{ij}^{2}}
and Lemma
3.12
below. Note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big(N\tilde{x}_{ij}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\sqrt{N}x_{ij}>N^{\vartheta}}\big)=\int_{N^{2\vartheta}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(|\sqrt{N}x_{ij}|^{2}>x)\mathrm{d}x\sim N^{\vartheta(2-\alpha)},

(3.17)

which gives
\sigma_{N}^{2}-(1-t)=\mathcal{O}(N^{\vartheta(2-\alpha)})
. The following lemma collects some useful properties of
\tilde{x}_{ij}
and the expansion for the characteristic function of
x_{ij}
.

Lemma 3.12
.

Then there exists constant
C>0
, such that

({i})

\tilde{x}_{ij}
’s are i.i.d. centered, with variance
\sigma_{N}^{2}/N
, third moment bound
N^{3/2}{\mathbb{E}}[|\tilde{x}_{ij}|^{3}]\leq CN^{\vartheta(3-\alpha)_{+}}
, and fourth moment bound
N^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[|\tilde{x}_{ij}|^{4}]\leq CN^{\vartheta(4-\alpha)}
,

({ii})

for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
\mathrm{Im}\,\lambda\leq 0
,

\displaystyle{\phi}_{N}(\lambda)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}\big(e^{-\mathrm{i}\lambda|{x}_{ij}|^{2}}\big)=1-\frac{\mathrm{i}(1-t)\lambda}{N}+c\frac{(\mathrm{i}\lambda)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}{N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}+\varepsilon_{N}(\lambda),\quad\text{and}\quad\varepsilon_{N}(\lambda)=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{|\lambda|^{(\alpha+\varrho)/2}}{N^{(\alpha+\varrho)/2}}\vee\frac{|\lambda|^{2}}{N^{2}}\Big).

Proof.

The proof of
(\mathrm{i})
is elementary.
To prove
(\mathrm{ii})
, we observe

1-\phi_{N}(\lambda)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\exp(-\mathrm{i}\lambda u/N)-1\big)\mathrm{d}F^{c}(u)=\frac{\mathrm{i}\lambda}{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-\mathrm{i}\lambda u/N)F^{c}(u)\mathrm{d}u,

where
F
be the distribution function of
Nx_{ij}^{2}
and let
F^{c}=1-F
.
Since
\int_{0}^{\infty}F^{c}(u)\mathrm{d}u=1-t
, we notice

1-\phi_{N}(\lambda)=\frac{\mathrm{i}\lambda(1-t)}{N}+\frac{\mathrm{i}\lambda}{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\exp(-\mathrm{i}\lambda u/N)-1\big)F^{c}(u)\mathrm{d}u.

The estimate
(\mathrm{ii})
can be obtained using the tail density assumption on
\sqrt{N}y_{ij}
(cf. Assumption
1.1
(i)).
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.11
.

Let
\hat{f}_{k}
be defined as
{f}_{k}
, but with the matrix
\tilde{X}
replaced by a matrix
\hat{X}
. The columns
\hat{X}_{i}
of
\hat{X}
are the same as those of
\tilde{X}
if
i\leq k
, but are independent random vectors with the same distribution as the columns of
\tilde{X}
if
i>k
. It is still valid to use the notation
{\mathbb{E}}_{k}
since
\tilde{X}
and
\hat{X}
share the same first
k
columns.
By the following elementary identity

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{k-1}\big(({\mathbb{E}}_{k}-{\mathbb{E}}_{k-1})(f_{k}(z))({\mathbb{E}}_{k}-{\mathbb{E}}_{k-1})(f_{k}(z^{\prime}))\big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{k}\big({\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k}(z)\hat{f}_{k}(z^{\prime}))\big)-\big({\mathbb{E}}_{k}{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}f_{k}(z)\big)\big({\mathbb{E}}_{k}{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\hat{f}_{k}(z^{\prime})\big),

(3.18)

it suffices to study the approximation for
{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}f_{k}(z)
and
{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}f_{k}(z)\hat{f}_{k}(z^{\prime})
. In the sequel, we write
f_{k}=f_{k}(z)
,
\hat{f}_{k}^{\prime}=\hat{f}_{k}(z^{\prime})
,
G_{k}=G(\tilde{X}^{(k)},z)
and
G^{\prime}_{k}=G(\tilde{X}^{(k)},z^{\prime})
for simplicity. By a minor process argument, for any
D>0
, there exists constant
C_{k}>0
such that
|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(\tilde{X}^{(k)}))-\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}|\geq C_{k}t^{2}
,
with probability at least
1-N^{-D}
.
This implies that there exists some constant
C_{k}>0
such that for any arbitrary large
D>0
,

\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\Omega}_{k}=\{\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(\tilde{X}^{(k)}))-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}\geq C_{k}t^{2}\})\geq 1-N^{-D}.

Then it is readily seen that
\mathrm{Re}\,[G(\tilde{X}^{(k)},z)]_{jj}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{k}}\geq 0
for any
z\in\{|z-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|\leq C_{k}t^{2}/10,|\mathrm{Im}\,z|\geq N^{-100}\}
. Since
\tilde{\Omega}_{k}
is independent of
\tilde{x}_{k}
, we can write

{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k})={\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k})\mathbf{1}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{k}}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k})\mathbf{1}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{k}^{c}}.

Using the facts that
|\tilde{x}_{jk}|\leq N^{1/\alpha+1/4+\epsilon_{\vartheta}}
and
|[G_{k}]_{jj}|\leq|\mathrm{Im}\,z|^{-1}\leq N^{101}
, we have for some large constant
K>0
such that

|{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k})\mathbf{1}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{k}^{c}}|\leq N^{K}\mathbf{1}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{k}^{c}}.

Next, we will mainly focus on the estimation for
{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k})\mathbf{1}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{k}}
. In the sequel, we omit the indicate function
\mathbf{1}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{k}}
from the display for simplicity, and keep in mind that all the estimates are done on the event
\tilde{\Omega}_{k}
.
Using the identity that for
w
with
\mathrm{Re}\,w>0
,
w^{-1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-sw}\mathrm{d}s
,
we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}f_{k}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\sum_{j}\tilde{x}_{jk}^{2}[G_{k}^{2}]_{jj}e^{-s\big(1+\sum_{j}\tilde{x}_{jk}^{2}[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)}\mathrm{d}s\Big)=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\partial_{z}\Big\{{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\Big(e^{-s\sum_{j}\tilde{x}_{jk}^{2}[G_{k}]_{jj}}\Big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s.

Recall
\tilde{\phi}_{N}
and
\phi_{N}
in Lemma
3.12
. We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}f_{k}=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\partial_{z}\Big\{\prod_{j}{\phi}_{N}\big(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s+\mathsf{Diff},

where

\mathsf{Diff}\coloneqq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\partial_{z}\Big\{\prod_{j}{\phi}_{N}\big(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)-\prod_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{N}\big(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s.

Note by the definition of
\tilde{x}_{jk}
’s for any
j\in[N]
, the following estimate holds uniformly for all
\lambda
with
\mathrm{Im}\,\lambda\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{N}(\lambda)-\tilde{\phi}_{N}(\lambda)\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(e^{-\mathrm{i}\lambda|x_{ij}|^{2}}-1\big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\sqrt{N}|x_{ij}|>N^{\vartheta}}\Big]\Big|\leq 2\mathbb{P}\big(\sqrt{N}|x_{ij}|>N^{\vartheta}\big)\lesssim N^{-\alpha\vartheta}.

Therefore, by a Cauchy integral argument with contour radius equals to
ct^{2}
for some sufficiently small
c>0
, we have for sufficiently large
K
,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{N^{-K}}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\partial_{z}\Big\{\prod_{j}{\phi}_{N}\big(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)-\prod_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{N}\big(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\lesssim t^{-2}N^{-\alpha\vartheta}\int_{N^{-K}}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim N^{1-\alpha/2-\epsilon}.

With the prescribe
K
, we also have

\displaystyle\Big|\int^{N^{-K}}_{0}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\partial_{z}\Big\{\prod_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{N}\big(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s\Big|=\Big|{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\Big(\int_{0}^{N^{-K}}\sum_{j}\tilde{x}_{jk}^{2}[G_{k}^{2}]_{jj}e^{-s\big(1+\sum_{j}\tilde{x}_{jk}^{2}[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big|\lesssim N^{-K/2},

and similar estimate holds if we replace
\tilde{\phi}_{N}
by
\phi_{N}
. Combining the above two displays, we can obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}f_{k}=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\partial_{z}\Big\{\prod_{j}\Big(1+\frac{1-t}{N}u_{j}(z,s)\Big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s+\mathcal{O}_{\prec}(N^{1-\alpha/2-\epsilon}),

where

\displaystyle u_{j}(z,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{1-t}\Big({\phi}\big(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)-1\Big)=-s[G_{k}]_{jj}+\mathsf{c}\frac{(s[G_{k}]_{jj})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}{N^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}(1-t)}+\frac{N}{1-t}\varepsilon_{N}(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj}).

We introduce the approximation
\mathsf{K}_{1}(z,s)
for the integrand as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{1}(z,s):=\frac{e^{-s-s(1-t){\mathrm{Tr}}G_{k}/N}}{s}\Big(1+\frac{\mathsf{c}}{N^{\alpha/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\big(s[G_{k}]_{jj}\big)^{\alpha/2}\Big).

Then our goal is to show on the event
\tilde{\Omega}_{k}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{z}\delta(z,s)\mathrm{d}s\lesssim N^{1-\alpha/2-\epsilon},

(3.19)

where
\delta(z,s)=\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\prod_{j}\big(1+\frac{1-t}{N}u_{j}(z,s)\big)-\mathsf{K}(z,s),
and
\epsilon>0
is a small constant.
By the Cauchy integral formula, we have
\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{z}\delta(z,s)\mathrm{d}s\Big|\lesssim t^{-2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\delta(z_{s},s)|\mathrm{d}s
,
where
z_{s}
is the maximizer of
|\delta(z,s)|
on the contour
\{z^{\prime}:|z^{\prime}-z|=C_{k}t^{2}/50\}
. To estimate the RHS of this inequality, we divide it into two parts,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\delta(z_{s},s)|\mathrm{d}s=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}|\delta(z_{s},s)|\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{N^{\varsigma}}^{\infty}|\delta(z_{s},s)|\mathrm{d}s=I_{1}+I_{2},

with
\varsigma
being chosen later . Using the fact that
[G_{k}]_{jj}\lesssim t^{-2}
on the event
\tilde{\Omega}_{k}
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2+\alpha}}\int_{N^{\varsigma}}^{\infty}s^{\alpha/2-1}e^{-s}\mathrm{d}s\leq e^{-N^{\varsigma}/3}.

For
I_{1}
, we further decompose it into three parts,

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}\Big|\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\Big(\prod_{j}\Big(1+\frac{1-t}{N}u_{j}(z_{s},s)\Big)-e^{\sigma_{N}^{2}/N\sum_{j}u_{j}(z_{s},s)}\Big)\Big|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}\Big|\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\Big(e^{(1-t)/N\sum_{j}u_{j}(z_{s},s)}-e^{-s(1-t){\mathrm{Tr}}G_{k}/N}\Big(1+\sum_{j}\mathsf{c}\frac{(s[G_{k}]_{jj})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}{N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}+\sum_{j}\varepsilon_{N}(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj})\Big)\Big)\Big|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}\Big|\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\Big(e^{-s(1-t){\mathrm{Tr}}G_{k}/N}\sum_{j}\varepsilon_{N}(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj})\Big)\Big|\mathrm{d}s=I_{11}+I_{12}+I_{13}.

Notice that on the event
\tilde{\Omega}_{k}
,

M_{s}=\max_{j}|u_{j}(z_{s},s)|\sigma_{N}^{2}\lesssim st^{-2},

and
\mathrm{Re}\,u_{j}(z_{s},s)=N(\mathrm{Re}\,\phi_{N}(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj})-1)\leq 0
.
Then using
[
16
, Lemma 4.5]
, we have on the event
\tilde{\Omega}_{k}
,

\displaystyle I_{11}\leq\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\cdot\frac{s^{2}}{Nt^{4}}e^{s^{2}/(Nt^{4})+\sum_{j}\mathrm{Re}\,((1-t)u_{j}(z_{s},s))/N}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\frac{1}{Nt^{6}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}e^{-s}se^{s^{2}/(Nt^{4})}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\frac{1}{Nt^{6}}.

By choosing
\varsigma<1/3
(say), we can obtain that

I_{11}\lesssim N^{-1}t^{-6}.

Applying the simple inequality that
|e^{x}-(1+x)|\leq 2|x|^{2}
for
|x|\leq 1/2
, we have

\displaystyle I_{12}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}\frac{e^{-s}}{s}\Big|\sum_{j}\mathsf{c}\frac{(s[G_{k}]_{jj})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}{N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}+\sum_{j}\varepsilon_{N}(-\mathrm{i}s[G_{k}]_{jj})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{\alpha-2}t^{2+2\alpha}}\int_{0}^{N^{\varsigma}}s^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim N^{\varsigma\alpha-\alpha+2}t^{-2-2\alpha}\lesssim N^{-3(\alpha-2)/5},

where in the last step, we chose
\varsigma<(\alpha-2)/(4\alpha)
. Finally, for
I_{13}
, we can use Lemma
3.12
(ii) to obtain that,

I_{13}\lesssim N^{-(\alpha-2)\vartheta}t^{-2-2\alpha}\lesssim N^{-3(\alpha-2)/4}.

Now we may conclude the proof of (
3.19
) by combining the above estimates and possibly adjusting the constants. This gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k})=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}_{1}(z,s)\mathrm{d}s+O_{\prec}(N^{1-\alpha/2-\epsilon}).

Similarly, we can obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(f_{k}\hat{f}_{k}^{\prime})=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{z}\partial_{z^{\prime}}\mathsf{K}_{2}(z,z^{\prime},s,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+O_{\prec}(N^{1-\alpha/2-\epsilon}),

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{2}(z,z^{\prime},s,s^{\prime})\coloneqq\frac{e^{-s-s^{\prime}-s(1-t){\mathrm{Tr}}G_{k}/N-s(1-t){\mathrm{Tr}}G^{\prime}_{k}/N}}{ss^{\prime}}\Big(1+\frac{\mathsf{c}}{N^{\alpha/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\big(s[G_{k}]_{jj}+s^{\prime}[G^{\prime}_{k}]_{jj}\big)^{\alpha/2}\Big).

Notice that the estimate in Proposition
2.12
can be obtained for our
G(\tilde{X}^{(k)},z)
as well in the same manner. Suppose that
\max\{|\mathcal{D}_{r}|,|\mathcal{D}_{c}|\}\leq N^{1-\epsilon_{d}}
for some
\epsilon_{d}
. Notice that
\epsilon_{d}\geq\epsilon_{\alpha}
by definition. Hence, the claim now follows by (i) employing (
3.18
), then substituting
\sigma_{N}^{2}[G_{k}]_{jj}(z)
with
{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{mp}}(z/\sigma_{N}^{2})
for
j\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
, and utilizing the bound
[G_{k}]_{jj}\prec 1/t
for
j\in\mathcal{D}_{r}
with the fact
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{d}/4}
; (ii) considering the estimates
\sigma_{N}^{2}-(1-t)=\mathcal{O}(N^{\vartheta(2-\alpha)})
(refer to Eqn. (
3.17
)),
\partial_{z}{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{mp}}(z/\sigma_{N}^{2})\sim t^{-1}
, and
\partial^{2}_{z}{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{mp}}(z/\sigma_{N}^{2})\sim t^{-3}
for
z
within the specified domain. This enables us to further replace
{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{mp}}(z/\sigma_{N}^{2})
and
{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{mp}}(z/\sigma_{N}^{2})
with
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z)
and
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z^{\prime})
, respectively.
∎

3.4.
Proof of Proposition
3.6

Let us first define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}(z):=\mathsf{c}N^{1-\alpha/2}c_{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s-sc_{N}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z)}\big(s\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z)\big)^{\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle m_{\mathsf{shift}}(z)\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{i}(\frac{z}{1-t}-c_{N}+1)\mathfrak{p}(z)}{2c_{N}z\sqrt{(\frac{z}{1-t}-\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}})(\lambda_{+}^{\mathsf{mp}}-\frac{z}{1-t})}}.

Then we have the following proposition concerning the expansion of
{\mathbb{E}}m_{X}(z)
.

Proposition 3.13
.

There exists some sufficiently small constant
\tau>0
, such that for any
z\in\{\zeta:|\zeta-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|\leq\tau t^{2},|\mathrm{Im}\,\zeta|\geq N^{-100}\}
, we have
m_{\mathsf{shift}}(z)=\mathcal{O}(t^{-1}N^{1-\alpha/2})
and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}m_{X}(z)=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z)+m_{\mathsf{shift}}(z)-\frac{\mathfrak{p}(z)}{2c_{N}z}+\mathcal{O}(N^{1-\alpha/2-\epsilon(4-\alpha)(\alpha-2)/50}).

(3.20)

Furthermore, for any
z\in\{\zeta:|\zeta-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|\ll t^{2},|\mathrm{Im}\,\zeta|\geq N^{-100}\}
,

\displaystyle tm_{\mathsf{shift}}(z)=\frac{\mathsf{c}N^{1-\alpha/2}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s-sc_{N}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}(\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}})}\big(s\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}(\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}})\big)^{\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}s}{2\sqrt{c_{N}}(1-\sqrt{c_{N}})}+\mathfrak{o}(N^{1-\alpha/2}).

(3.21)

Proof.

By the resolvent expansion, we have for any
z\in\{\zeta:|\zeta-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|\leq\tau t^{2},|\mathrm{Im}\,\zeta|\geq N^{-100}\}
,

\displaystyle\big[G(X^{\top},z)\big]_{ii}=-\big(z+zx_{i}^{\top}G(X^{(i)},z)x_{i}\big)^{-1}.

(3.22)

Let
Q=Q_{\mathsf{diag}}+Q_{\mathsf{off}}
with
Q_{\mathsf{diag}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{M}x_{ji}^{2}\big[G(X^{(i)},z)\big]_{jj}
and
Q_{\mathsf{off}}:=\sum_{\ell\neq k}x_{ki}x_{\ell i}\big[G(X^{(i)},z)\big]_{k\ell}
.
Then, we can rewrite (
3.22
) as:

\displaystyle\big[G(X^{\top},z)\big]_{ii}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{z(1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}})}+\frac{Q_{\mathsf{off}}}{z(1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}})^{2}}-\frac{Q_{\mathsf{off}}^{2}}{z(1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}})^{2}(1+Q)}.

(3.23)

Taking expectation at both sides gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[G(X^{\top},z)\big]_{ii}=-\frac{1}{z}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}}}\Big]-\frac{1}{z}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{Q_{\mathsf{off}}^{2}}{(1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}})^{2}(1+Q)}\Big]=I_{1}+I_{2},

where the second term at the right hand side of (
3.23
) vanished due to symmetry.
Notice that when
\Psi^{(i)}
is good, we have w.h.p. that

\displaystyle|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}({X}^{(i)}))-z|

\displaystyle=|(1-t)\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|-|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}({X}^{(i)}))-(1-t)\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}|-|\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}-z|

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{c_{N}}t^{2}-\sqrt{c_{N}}t^{2}/4-\tau t^{2}\geq\sqrt{c_{N}}t^{2}/2,

where in the last step we used the fact that
|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}({X}^{(i)}))-(1-t)\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}|\leq N^{-\epsilon_{b}}
w.h.p., and we also chose
\tau<\sqrt{c_{N}}t^{2}/4
. This together with the fact that
\Psi^{(i)}
is good w.h.p. gives
\mathbb{P}\big(\Omega_{i}=\big\{|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}({X}^{(i)}))-z|\geq\sqrt{c_{N}}t^{2}/2\big\}\big)\geq 1-N^{-D}
.
Notice that
\mathrm{Re}\,Q_{\mathsf{diag}}\geq 0
and
\mathrm{Re}\,Q\geq 0
hold on
\Omega_{i}
.
Then for
I_{2}
, with the smallness of
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{i}^{c})
, we have
I_{2}={\mathbb{E}}[{Q_{\mathsf{off}}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}}/[(1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}})^{2}(1+Q)]]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-D})
.
We then bound
I_{2}
as

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq{\mathbb{E}}|Q_{\mathsf{off}}|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-D})=2N^{-2}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X^{(i)},z)\overline{G(X^{(i)},z)}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}\Big]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1})=\mathcal{O}_{\prec}(t^{-4}N^{-1}).

Next, we estimate
I_{1}
.
Due to the smallness of
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{i}^{c})
, we only have to do the estimation on the event
\Omega_{i}
. Specially, we have
I_{1}=-{\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}/(z+zQ_{\mathsf{diag}})]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-D})
.
Notice that
\mathrm{Re}\,Q_{\mathsf{diag}}\geq 0
on the event
\Omega_{i}
. Using the identity that for
w
with
\mathrm{Re}\,w>0
,
w^{-1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-sw}\mathrm{d}s
and setting
w=1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{z}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s(1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}})}\mathrm{d}s\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}\Big]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-D})=-\frac{1}{z}{\mathbb{E}}\Big({\mathbb{E}}_{x_{i}}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s(1+Q_{\mathsf{diag}})}\mathrm{d}s\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-D})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{z}{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\prod_{j}\phi_{N}\big(-\mathrm{i}s\big[G(X^{(i)},z)\big]_{jj}\big)\mathrm{d}s\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-D}).

Then we may proceed as the estimation in the proof of Proposition
3.11
to obtain that

\displaystyle I_{1}=-\frac{1}{z}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{1+(1-t){\mathrm{Tr}}G(X^{(i)},z)/N}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{i}}\Big]-\frac{\mathfrak{p}(z)}{z}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-3(\alpha-2)/5}).

Further using the
\mathcal{O}_{\prec}(t^{-4}N^{-1})
bound for
\mathrm{Var}(M^{-1}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X^{(i)},z))
and the fact
{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X^{(i)},z)-{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X,z)\prec t^{-4}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle I_{1}=-\frac{1}{z}\Big(\frac{1}{1+(1-t){\mathbb{E}}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X,z)/N}\Big)-\frac{\mathfrak{p}(z)}{z}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-3(\alpha-2)/5})+\mathcal{O}_{\prec}(t^{-4}N^{-1}).

Collecting the estimates for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, and then summing over
i
, we have

\displaystyle N^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X^{\top},z)=-\frac{1}{z}\Big(\frac{1}{1+(1-t){\mathbb{E}}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X,z)/N}\Big)-\frac{\mathfrak{p}(z)}{z}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-3(\alpha-2)/5}).

Using the simple equation
{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X,z)-{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X^{\top},z)=(N-M)/z
, the above equation can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle c_{N}{\mathbb{E}}m_{X}(z)=-\frac{1}{z}\Big(\frac{1}{1+(1-t)c_{N}{\mathbb{E}}m_{X}(z)}\Big)-\frac{\mathfrak{p}(z)+1-c_{N}}{z}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-3(\alpha-2)/5}).

(3.24)

Notice that for
z=\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}+\mathrm{i}N^{-100K_{\zeta}}
, we have

(\frac{z}{1-t}+c_{N}-1)^{2}-\frac{4c_{N}z}{1-t}=\frac{c_{N}t^{2}(2-t)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-90K_{\zeta}}).

Then by continuity, we may choose
\tau
sufficiently small such that for any
z\in\{\zeta:|\zeta-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|\leq\tau t^{2},|\mathrm{Im}\,\zeta|\geq N^{-100K_{\zeta}}\}
, we have
(\frac{z}{1-t}+c_{N}-1)^{2}-\frac{4c_{N}z}{1-t}\sim t^{2}
.
Having this bound, we may solve the quadric equation (
3.24
) and then compare it with (
1.2
) to obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}m_{X}(z)=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z)+m_{\mathsf{shift}}(z)-\frac{\mathfrak{p}(z)}{2c_{N}z}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-11(\alpha-2)/20}),

which proves (
3.20
).
Using the fact that
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}+\mathrm{i}N^{-100K_{\zeta}})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}(\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}})\leq t
, we may further derive that

\displaystyle tm_{\mathsf{shift}}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}+\mathrm{i}N^{-100})=\frac{\mathsf{c}N^{1-\alpha/2}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s-sc_{N}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}(\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}})}\big(s\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}(\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}})\big)^{\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}s}{2\sqrt{c_{N}}(1-\sqrt{c_{N}})}+\mathcal{O}(tN^{1-\alpha/2}).

This together with the crude bound
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}+\mathrm{i}N^{-100K_{\zeta}})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})=\mathcal{O}(N^{-90K_{\zeta}})
proves (
3.21
), which completes the proof of Proposition
3.13
.
∎

The following corollary is a direct consequence of Proposition
3.13
.

Corollary 3.14
.

Let
\tau
be chosen as in Proposition
3.13
. Then for any
z\in\{\zeta:|\zeta-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|\leq\tau t^{2}/2,|\mathrm{Im}\,\zeta|\geq N^{-100}\}
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}m^{(k)}_{X}(z)-(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(z))^{(k)}=\mathcal{O}(t^{-(2k+1)}N^{1-\alpha/2}),

Proof.

The claim follows from Proposition
3.13
with Cauchy integral. We omit further details.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.6
.

Replacing
{\mathbb{E}}[m_{X}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})]
by
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})
in the expression of
\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\Phi}_{t}({\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})+\big(2c_{N}t\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+O(t^{2})\big)\cdot\big(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})-{\mathbb{E}}[m_{X}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})]\big)+\mathcal{O}(|\zeta_{\mathsf{e}}-\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}|).

(3.25)

Expanding
\bar{\Phi}_{t}({\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})
around
\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}
and using the fact that
\bar{\Phi}^{\prime}_{t}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})=0
, we have that there exists
\tilde{\zeta}\in[\bar{\zeta}_{-,t},{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}]
such that

\bar{\Phi}_{t}({\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})=\bar{\Phi}_{t}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})+\bar{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{t}(\tilde{\zeta})({\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})^{2}=\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+\bar{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{t}(\tilde{\zeta})({\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})^{2}.

Substituting this expansion back into (
3.25
), and using the bound in Corollary
3.14
, (
3.25
) becomes

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}=\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+2c_{N}t\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}\big(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})-{\mathbb{E}}[m_{X}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})]\big)+\bar{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{t}(\tilde{\zeta})({\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})^{2}+\mathfrak{o}(N^{1-\alpha/2}).

Note by considering that
\tilde{\zeta}-(1-t)\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}\sim t^{2}
, it can be easily verified that
\bar{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{t}(\tilde{\zeta})\sim t^{-2}
.

By employing Corollary
3.14
along with the variance bounds for
m_{X}^{(k)}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})
in Lemma
3.4
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}^{(k)}_{m}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})\coloneqq m_{X}^{(k)}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})-(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}))^{(k)}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(N^{-1/2+\epsilon/2}t^{-2-k}+N^{1-\alpha/2}t^{-2k-1}).

With the above probabilistic bounds in place, we may now proceed to follow the expansion detailed in the proof of Lemma
3.4
, but this time substitute
\zeta_{\mathsf{e}}
with
\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}
and
{\mathbb{E}}(m_{X}^{(k)}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}))
with
\bar{m}^{(k)}_{X}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})
(cf. (
3.11
)-(
3.16
)). It becomes evident that the
\mathsf{ZOT}_{\zeta}
therein vanishes due to the fact that
\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{\prime}(\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})=0
. This eventually leads to

\bar{\Delta}_{\zeta}\coloneqq\zeta_{-,t}-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(N^{-1/2+\epsilon/2}+N^{1-\alpha/2}).

Therefore, with
{\Delta}_{\zeta}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(N^{-1/2+\epsilon/2}t^{6})
, we have

\bar{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{t}(\tilde{\zeta})({\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t})^{2}\sim t^{-2}(\bar{\Delta}_{\zeta}-{\Delta}_{\zeta})^{2}=\mathfrak{o}(N^{1-\alpha/2}).

Consequently, we arrive at

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}=\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+2c_{N}t\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}\big(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}^{(t)}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})-{\mathbb{E}}m_{X}(\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}})\big)+\mathfrak{o}(N^{1-\alpha/2}).

Recalling from (
3.7
) that
\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}-{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}\prec N^{-\beta/2}t^{2}
, we can deduce that
\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}-\hat{\zeta}_{\mathsf{e}}\prec N^{-\beta/2}t^{2}
. The claim now follows by (
3.21
) in Proposition
3.13
and the fact
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{mp}}(\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}})=(\sqrt{c_{N}}-c_{N})^{-1}
.
∎

4.
Beyond Gaussian divisible model

In this section, we present three Green function function comparison results, as we mentioned in the Section
1
. Their proofs will be postponed to the next section. Recall the notations in (
1.14
).

4.1.
Entry-wise bound

We first introduce the following shorthand notation: for any
a,b\in[M]
and
u,v\in[N]
,

\mathfrak{X}_{ab}=\mathfrak{X}_{ab}(\Psi)\coloneqq\begin{cases}1&\text{if }a\text{ or }b\in\mathcal{T}_{r},\\
t^{2}&\text{if }a\in\mathcal{D}_{r},b\in\mathcal{D}_{r}\end{cases},\quad\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}=\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}(\Psi)\coloneqq\begin{cases}1&\text{if }u\text{ or }v\in\mathcal{T}_{c},\\
t^{2}&\text{if }u\in\mathcal{D}_{c},v\in\mathcal{D}_{c}\end{cases},

\mathfrak{Z}_{au}=\mathfrak{Z}_{au}(\Psi)\coloneqq\begin{cases}1&\text{if }a\in\mathcal{T}_{r}\text{ or }u\in\mathcal{T}_{c}\\
t^{2}&\text{if }a\in\mathcal{D}_{r},u\in\mathcal{D}_{c}\end{cases}.

Proposition 4.1

(Entry-wise bound)
.

Recall
\mathsf{D}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})
defined in (
1.24
). Let
\mathsf{D}_{\leq}=\{z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathsf{D}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}):\eta\leq N^{-\varepsilon}\}
. Set
10\epsilon_{a}\leq\varepsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon_{b}/500
, and set
\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}
sufficiently small, and
3\epsilon_{1}<\varepsilon\leq\epsilon_{b}/100
. Suppose that
\Psi
is good. Let
\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}
be the probability conditioned on the event that the
(\psi_{ij})
matrix is a given
\Psi
. Suppose that
\Psi
is good (cf. (
2.4
)). Then for each
\delta>0
and
D>0
, there exists a large constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}\sup_{z\in\mathsf{D}_{\leq}}\sup_{a,b\in[M]}

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{X}_{ab}[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab}|\vee\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}\sup_{z\in\mathsf{D}_{\leq}}\sup_{u,v\in[N]}|\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z)]_{uv}|

\displaystyle\vee\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}\sup_{z\in\mathsf{D}_{\leq}}\sup_{a\in[M],u\in[N]}|\mathfrak{Z}_{au}[G^{\gamma}(z)Y^{\gamma}]_{au}|\geq N^{\delta}\Big)\leq CN^{-D},

The proof of Proposition
4.1
follows a similar approach to the one demonstrated in
[
5
, Proposition 3.17]
. It relies on the entry-wise bounds for the Green functions of
Y^{0}
as provided in Theorem
2.10
, which serve as an input for the subsequent comparison theorem. We defer the proof to Section
5.2
.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
F:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\mu\leq d}F^{(\mu)}(x)\leq(|x|+1)^{C_{0}},\qquad\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\mu\leq d\\
|x|\leq 2N^{2}\end{subarray}}F^{(\mu)}(x)\leq N^{C_{0}},

for some real number
C_{0},d>0
. For any
0-1
matrix
\Psi
and complex number
z
, we define for any
a,b\in[M]
and
u,v\in[N]
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{0,ab}=\mathfrak{I}_{0,ab}(\Psi,z)\coloneqq\max_{0\leq\mu\leq d}\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|F^{(\mu)}(\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab})\big|\big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{1,uv}=\mathfrak{I}_{1,uv}(\Psi,z)\coloneqq\max_{0\leq\mu\leq d}\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|F^{(\mu)}(\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}\mathrm{Im}\,[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z)]_{uv})\big|\big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{2,au}=\mathfrak{I}_{2,au}(\Psi,z)\coloneqq\max_{0\leq\mu\leq d}\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|F^{(\mu)}(\mathfrak{Z}_{au}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)Y^{\gamma}]_{au})\big|\big),

and
\Omega=\Omega_{0}\cap\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega_{w}
,
Q_{0}=Q_{0}(\varepsilon,z)\coloneqq 1-\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\big(\Omega\big)
with

\displaystyle\Omega_{0}=\Omega_{0}(\varepsilon,z)\coloneqq\Big\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in[M]\\
0\leq\gamma\leq 1\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{X}_{ab}[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab}|\leq N^{\varepsilon}\Big\},\Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}(\varepsilon,z)\coloneqq\Big\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,v\in[N]\\
0\leq\gamma\leq 1\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z)]_{uv}|\leq N^{\varepsilon}\Big\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}(\varepsilon,z)\coloneqq\Big\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in[M],u\in[N]\\
0\leq\gamma\leq 1\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{Z}_{au}[G^{\gamma}(z)Y^{\gamma}]_{au}|\leq N^{\varepsilon}\Big\},\Omega_{w}=\Omega_{w}(\varepsilon)\coloneqq\Big\{\sup_{i\in[M],j\in[N]}|w_{ij}|\leq N^{-1/2+\varepsilon}t\Big\}.

Suppose that
\Psi
is good. There exist sufficiently small positive constants
\varepsilon\leq\epsilon_{b}/100
and
\omega
, and a large constant
C>0
such that for

\displaystyle(\#_{1},\#_{2},\#_{3})\in\{

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab},\;\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{0}(z)]_{ab},\;\mathfrak{I}_{0,ab}),

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}\mathrm{Im}\,[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z)]_{uv},\;\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}\mathrm{Im}\,[\mathcal{G}^{0}(z)]_{uv},\;\mathfrak{I}_{1,uv}),

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{Z}_{au}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)Y^{\gamma}]_{au},\;\mathfrak{Z}_{au}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{0}(z)Y^{0}]_{au},\;\mathfrak{I}_{2,au})\},

we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}\big|{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F(\#_{1})\big)

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F(\#_{2})\big)\big|<CN^{-\omega}(\#_{3}+1)+CQ_{0}N^{C+C_{0}},

(4.1)

for any
a,b\in[M]
and
u,v\in[N]
. The same estimates hold if
\mathrm{Im}\,
’s are replaced by
\mathrm{Re}\,
’s.

4.2.
Average local law

In this section, we write
m^{\gamma}(z)=m_{Y^{\gamma}}(z)
,
G^{\gamma}(z)=G(Y^{\gamma},z)
, and
\bar{G}^{\gamma}(z)=G(Y^{\gamma},\bar{z})
for simplicity. Let
z_{t}:=\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta
. Then we have the following theorem.

Theorem 4.3
.

Suppose that
\Psi
is good. Let us define
z_{t}\coloneqq\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta
. We assume that
\eta\in[N^{-\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon},N^{-\frac{2}{3}}]
,
E\in[-N^{-\varepsilon_{1}},N^{-\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon}]
for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Then there exists a constant
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all integer
p\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|N\eta\big(\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z_{t})-\mathrm{Im}\,\tilde{m}^{0}(z_{t})\big)\big|^{2p}\big)\leq(1+\mathfrak{o}(1)){\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|N\eta\big(\mathrm{Im}\,m^{0}(z_{t})-\mathrm{Im}\,\tilde{m}^{0}(z_{t})\big)\big|^{2p}\big)+N^{-\delta_{0}p},

where
\tilde{m}^{0}(z)=m_{X+t^{1/2}\tilde{W}}(z)
. Here
\tilde{W}
is an i.i.d. copy of
W
and it is also independent of
X
. Further, the same estimate holds if
\mathrm{Im}\,
’s are replaced by
\mathrm{Re}\,
’s.

The above comparison inequality directly leads to the following theorem, which is crucial for the rigidity estimate for the
\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))
, serving as a key component in proving the universality result.

Theorem 4.4

(Rigidity estimate)
.

Suppose
\Omega_{\Psi}
holds. Then, with high probability,

|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{-,t}|\leq N^{-2/3+\epsilon}.

Proof.

By Markov’s inequality, Theorem
4.3
and the following local law for
m^{0}

\displaystyle|m^{0}(\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta)-m_{t}(\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta)|\prec\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{N\eta},&E\geq 0,\\
\\
\frac{1}{N(|E|+\eta)}+\frac{1}{(N\eta)^{2}\sqrt{|E|+\eta}},&E\leq 0,\end{array}\right.

we can obtain (
4.2
) with
m^{0}
replaced by
m^{1}
and further for the case
E\leq 0
the following

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,m^{1}(\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta)-\mathrm{Im}\,m_{t}(\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta)\prec\frac{1}{N(|E|+\eta)}+\frac{1}{(N\eta)^{2}\sqrt{|E|+\eta}}+\frac{1}{N^{1+\delta_{0}/2}\eta}.

(4.5)

We remark here that the local law in (
4.2
) has been proved in
[
23
]
around the right edge for the deformed rectangular matrices, under the assumption that the original rectangular matrices satisfy the
\eta_{\ast}
-regularity. The argument can be adapted to our model, but around the left edge, again with the
\eta_{\ast}
-regularity as the input. The derivation is almost the same, and thus we do not reproduce it here.

Further, similarly to Lemma
2.8
, we can prove

|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}|\prec N^{-2\epsilon_{b}}
and
|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}|\prec N^{-2\epsilon_{b}}.

By (
4.2
), and the crude lower bound on
\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))
implied by
[
63
]
, we also have

|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{-,t}|\prec N^{-\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{-,t}|\prec N^{-2\epsilon_{b}}.

(4.6)

With the aid of the
m^{1}
analogue of (
4.2
), (
4.5
) and (
4.6
), the remaining reasoning is routine and thus we omit it; see the proof of Theorem 1.4 in
[
36
]
, for instance.
∎

4.3.
Green function comparison for edge universality

Theorem 4.5

(Green function comparison)
.

Let
F:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function whose derivatives satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{x}|F^{\alpha}(x)|(|x|+1)^{-C_{1}}\leq C_{1},\quad\alpha=1,\cdots,d

for some constant
C_{1}>0
and sufficiently large integer
d>0
. Let
\Psi
be good. Then there exist
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta_{1}>0
depending on
\epsilon_{a}
such that for any
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
,
N\geq N_{0}
and real numbers
E,E_{1}
and
E_{2}
satisfying
|E|,\;|E_{1}|,\;|E_{2}|\;\leq N^{-2/3+\epsilon}
,
\eta_{0}=N^{-2/3-\epsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[F\Big(N\int_{E_{1}}^{E_{2}}\mathrm{Im}\,m^{1}(\lambda_{-,t}+y+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0})\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[F\Big(N\int_{E_{1}}^{E_{2}}\mathrm{Im}\,m^{0}(\lambda_{-,t}+y+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0})\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\Big]\Big|\leq CN^{-\delta_{1}},

(4.7)

for some constant
C>0
, and in the case
\alpha=8/3
, (
4.7
) holds with
\lambda_{-,t}
replaced by
\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}
.

Employing the above comparison inequality along with the rigidity estimate int Theorem
4.4
, we can deduce the following universality result around the random edge
\lambda_{-,t}
(and deterministic edge
\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}
if
\alpha=8/3
), whose proof will be stated in the Appendix
B.2
.

Corollary 4.6
.

For all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{-,t})\leq s\Big)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{-,t})\leq s\Big).

(4.8)

Moreover, if
\alpha=8/3
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}})\leq s\Big)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}})\leq s\Big).

(4.9)

Now we can prove our main theorem: Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

The conclusions (i)-(iii) in Theorem
1.2
follows from (
4.8
) in Corollary
4.6
and Theorem
2.14
. To prove the critical case when
\alpha=8/3
, i.e., (iv), from (
2.12
) in Theorem
2.13
, it is easy to show that the distribution of
\lambda_{-,t}
is asymptotically independent of the fluctuation of
\lambda_{M}\big(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{-,t}
since the former is a function of
X
only. It can be shown by a standard characteristic function argument that for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\gamma_{N}M^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}\big(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}\big)\leq s\Big)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(M^{2/3}(\mu_{M}^{\mathsf{GOE}}+2+\gamma_{N}\mathcal{X}_{\alpha})\leq s\Big).

where in the RHS
\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}
is independent of
\mathsf{GOE}
. Then further,
together with the comparison (
4.9
) we conclude (iv). Hence, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
∎

5.
Proofs for the Green function comparisons

In this section, we will mainly prove the Green function comparisons stated in the last section. We will show the details for Theorems
4.2
and
4.3
only. The proof of Theorem
4.5
is similar to Theorem
4.3
, and thus will only be discussed briefly here and the details are stated in the Appendix
B.3
.

5.1.
Some further notations

Let us introduce some additional notations.
We denote by
\mathsf{E}_{(ij)}
the standard basis for
\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
, i.e.,
[\mathsf{E}_{(ij)}]_{ab}\coloneqq\delta_{ia}\delta_{jb}
.
Replacement matrix notation: For any
A\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
, the replacement matrix
A_{(ij)}^{\lambda}=A_{(ij)}(\lambda)\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
is defined as,

\big[A_{(ij)}(\lambda)\big]_{ab}:=\begin{cases}\lambda&\text{if }(i,j)=(a,b)\\
A_{ab}&\text{if }(i,j)\neq(a,b)\end{cases},\quad a\in[M],\quad b\in[N].

(5.1)

Let
G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}(z):=(\mathcal{S}(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda})-z)^{-1}
be the resolvent of
\mathcal{S}(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda})
with
Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}=(Y^{\gamma})_{(ij)}({\lambda})
. We define

\displaystyle d_{ij}(\gamma,w_{ij}):=\gamma(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}+\chi_{ij}b_{ij}+(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}t^{1/2}w_{ji},

\displaystyle e_{ij}(\gamma,w_{ij}):=c_{ij}+(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}t^{1/2}w_{ij},\qquad i\in[M],\quad j\in[N].

(5.2)

In the sequel, for brevity, we also write
\sum_{i,j}=\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{N}
.

5.2.
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Let us prove Proposition
4.1
assuming that Theorem
4.2
holds. The proof of Theorem
4.2
is deferred to the next subsection.
For
\delta>0
and
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathsf{D}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})
(cf. (
1.24
)), we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{0}(\delta,z,\Psi)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}\sup_{a,b\in[M]}|z^{1/2}\mathfrak{X}_{ab}[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab}|>N^{\delta}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{1}(\delta,z,\Psi)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}\sup_{u,v\in[N]}|z^{1/2}\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z)]_{uv}|>N^{\delta}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{2}(\delta,z,\Psi)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{0\leq\gamma\leq 1}\sup_{a\in[M],u\in[N]}|\mathfrak{Z}_{au}[G^{\gamma}(z)Y^{\gamma}]_{au}|>N^{\delta}\Big).

The following monotonicity lemma will be a useful tool.

Lemma 5.1
.

Suppose that
\Psi
is good. Fix
\varepsilon
and
\omega
as in Theorem
4.2
.
For all
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathsf{D}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})
, we set
z^{\prime}=E^{\prime}+\mathrm{i}\eta^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle E^{\prime}=E+\frac{(1-N^{\varepsilon/3})(\sqrt{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}-E)}{2},\quad\eta^{\prime}=N^{\varepsilon/6}\eta.

(5.3)

Then for any
\delta>0
and
D>0
, there exists a large constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\{0,1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\delta,z,\Psi)\leq CN^{C}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\{0,1,2\}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z^{\prime},\Psi)+CN^{-D}.

(5.4)

Proof.

This is a minor modification of
[
5
, Lemma 4.3]
. The proof requires Theorem
4.2
. For brevity, the detail is provided in the Appendx
B.1
.
∎

With the above lemma, we can prove Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

The proof is similar to the proof of Proposition 3.17 in
[
5
]
. Let
\varepsilon
be as in Theorem
4.2
. It follows from Lemma
5.1
that for any
z_{0}=\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+E_{0}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}\in\mathsf{D}(2\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})
and
\eta_{0}\leq N^{-\varepsilon}
, we may find
z_{1}=\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+E_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{1}
defined through (
5.3
) such that for any
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\delta,z_{0},\Psi)\leq C_{1}N^{C_{1}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{1},\Psi)+C_{1}N^{-D}.

(5.5)

Now it suffices to bound
\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{1},\Psi)
. Notice that for
\varepsilon>3\varepsilon_{1}

\displaystyle|E_{1}|\lesssim|E_{0}|+N^{\varepsilon/3}|\eta_{0}|\lesssim N^{-2\epsilon_{1}}+N^{-2/3\varepsilon}\ll N^{-\varepsilon_{1}}.

This means that
z_{1}\in(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})
. Applying Lemma
5.1
again with
\delta=\varepsilon/2
, we can find
z_{2}=\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+E_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{2}

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{1},\Psi)\leq C_{2}N^{C_{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{2},\Psi)+C_{2}N^{-D},

where
\eta_{2}=N^{\varepsilon/6}\eta_{1}
and
|E_{2}|\ll N^{-\varepsilon_{1}}
. We may now repeat the above procedure until
z_{m}=\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}+E_{m}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{m}
with
\eta_{m}\geq KN^{-\varepsilon/2}
for some sufficiently large
K
. It can be computed that

\displaystyle\eta_{m}\lesssim N^{-\varepsilon/2}N^{\varepsilon/6}=N^{-\varepsilon/3},\quad\text{and}\quad|E_{m}|\lesssim|E_{0}|+\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}N^{\varepsilon/3}\eta_{i},\quad\eta_{i}=N^{\varepsilon i/6}\eta_{0}.

This implies that
|E_{m}|\lesssim|E_{0}|+N^{-\varepsilon/2}\ll N^{-\varepsilon_{1}}
. Then using the fact that
\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{m},\Psi)=0
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\delta,z_{0},\Psi)\leq C_{1}N^{C_{1}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{1},\Psi)+C_{1}N^{-D}\leq C_{m}N^{-D}.

The claim now follows by adjusting constants.
∎

5.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.2

We need the following elementary resolvent expansion formula.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any deterministic matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
, let its linearisation
\mathcal{L}(A)
be defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(A)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&A\\
A^{\top}&0\\
\end{array}\right).

Let
\mathcal{R}(A,z)=(z^{1/2}\mathcal{L}(A)-z)^{-1}
be the resolvent of
\mathcal{L}(A)
. The Schur complement formula also gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(A,z)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}G(A,z)&z^{-1/2}G(A,z)A\\
z^{-1/2}A^{\top}G(A,z)&G(A^{\top},z)\\
\end{array}\right).

Then for any
B=A+\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
, we have for any integer
s\geq 0

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(A,z)=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\big(\mathcal{R}(B,z)\mathcal{L}(z^{1/2}\Delta)\big)^{j}\mathcal{R}(B,z)+\big(\mathcal{R}(B,z)\mathcal{L}(z^{1/2}\Delta)\big)^{s+1}\mathcal{R}(A,z).

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

During the proof, we omit the
z
dependence and write
d_{ij}=d_{ij}(\gamma,w_{ij})
and
e_{ij}=e_{ij}(\gamma,w_{ij})
for simplicity. We only show the proof for
(\#_{1},\#_{2},\#_{3})=(\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab},\;\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{0}(z)]_{ab},\;\mathfrak{I}_{0,ab})
with
a\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
or
b\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
, and the others can be proved similarly.
Observing that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F([\mathrm{Im}\,G^{\gamma}]_{ab})\big)}{\partial\gamma}=-\sum_{i,j}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[F^{(1)}(\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}]_{ab})\mathrm{Im}\,\big([G^{\gamma}]_{ib}[G^{\gamma}Y^{\gamma}]_{aj}\big)\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,j}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[F^{(1)}(\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}]_{ab})\mathrm{Im}\,\big([G^{\gamma}]_{ai}[(Y^{\gamma})^{\top}G^{\gamma}]_{jb}\big)\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big]=-\sum_{i,j}\Big[(I)_{ij}+(II)_{ij}\Big],

and therefore it suffices to show that there exists some constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\Big[|(I)_{ij}|+|(II)_{ij}|\Big]\leq\frac{C}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\big(N^{-\omega}(\mathfrak{I}_{0,ab}+1)+Q_{0}N^{C_{0}+C}\big).

(5.8)

We will focus on the estimation for
(I)_{ij}
’s , while the estimates for the
(II)_{ij}
’s can be handled in an identical fanshion.
To ease the presentation, we further define the shorthand notation

\displaystyle f_{(ij)}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=U_{(ij)}(\lambda)V_{(ij)}(\lambda),

\displaystyle U_{(ij)}(\lambda)=F^{(1)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}\big]_{ab}\big),

\displaystyle\quad V_{(ij)}(\lambda)=\mathrm{Im}\,\big([G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}]_{ib}[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}]_{aj}\big).

We also define
\tilde{V}_{(ij)}(\lambda)=\mathrm{Im}\,\big([G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}]_{ai}[(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda})^{\top}G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda}]_{jb}\big)
.
Then for any
i\in[M],j\in[N]
,
(I)_{ij}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle(I)_{ij}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[f_{(ij)}([Y^{\gamma}]_{ij})\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\cdot(\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1})\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[f_{(ij)}(d_{ij})\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ast)}{=}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[f_{(ij)}(d_{ij})\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}-\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}t^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[w_{ij}f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}

\displaystyle=(J_{1})_{ij}-(J_{2})_{ij},

where in
(\ast)
, we used the fact that
\mathsf{A}_{ij}=0
if
\psi_{ij}=1
.

Let us consider
(J_{2})_{ij}
first. Applying Gaussian integration by parts on
w_{ij}
, we have

\displaystyle|(J_{2})_{ij}|=\Big|\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}N}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\partial_{w_{ij}}f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\Big]\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}N}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|\partial_{w_{ij}}f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\big|\Big]\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}+\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}N}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|\partial_{w_{ij}}f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{c}}\big|\Big]\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\partial_{w_{ij}}f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})=U_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\cdot\partial_{w_{ij}}V_{(ij)}(e_{ij})+V_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\cdot\partial_{w_{ij}}U_{(ij)}(e_{ij}),

(5.9)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{w_{ij}}U_{(ij)}(e_{ij})=-(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}t^{1/2}F^{(2)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)\big(V_{(ij)}(e_{ij})+\tilde{V}_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\big),

\displaystyle\partial_{w_{ij}}V_{(ij)}(e_{ij})=-(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}t^{1/2}\mathrm{Im}\,\Big(\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ii}\big[(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}})^{\top}G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{jb}[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{aj}

\displaystyle\quad+\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ij}\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ib}[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{aj}-[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{ib}[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{ai}

\displaystyle\quad+[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{ib}[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{ai}[(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}})^{\top}G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{jj}+[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{ib}[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{aj}[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}]_{ij}\Big).

(5.10)

When
\psi_{ij}=1
, we have
i\in\mathcal{D}_{r}
and
j\in\mathcal{D}_{c}
. Then
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}|V_{(ij)}(e_{ij})|\leq N^{2\varepsilon}t^{-2}
and
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}|\partial_{w_{ij}}V_{(ij)}(e_{ij})|\leq N^{3\varepsilon}t^{-7/2}
.
Therefore, we may find a large constant
K_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle|(J_{2})_{ij}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}}{N^{1-3\varepsilon}t^{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|F^{(1)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)\big|\Big]+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}}{N^{1-4\varepsilon}t^{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|F^{(2)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{t^{1/2}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}}{N}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|\partial_{w_{ij}}f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{c}}\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}}{N^{1-3\varepsilon}t^{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|F^{(1)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)\big|\Big]+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}}{N^{1-4\varepsilon}t^{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|F^{(2)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)\big|\Big]+N^{K_{1}}Q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1},

where in the second step, we used the crude bound that
|\partial_{w_{ij}}f_{(ij)}(e_{ij})|\leq N^{K_{1}}
for some sufficiently large
K_{1}
, which can be obtained by the fact that
\mathrm{Im}\,z>N^{-1}
. By the facts that
\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}\leq N^{1-\epsilon_{\alpha}}
and
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{\alpha}/4}
, we can choose
\varepsilon<\epsilon_{\alpha}/16
to obtain that

\displaystyle|(J_{2})_{ij}|\lesssim\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}}{N^{1-\epsilon_{\alpha}/2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\big|F^{(1)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)\big|+\big|F^{(2)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)\big|\Big]+N^{K_{1}}Q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}.

Next, we consider
(J_{1})_{ij}
. Recall that
d_{ij}=\gamma(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}+\chi_{ij}b_{ij}+(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}t^{1/2}w_{ji}
. Applying Taylor expansion on
f(d_{ij})
around
0
, for an
s_{1}
to be chosen later, we have

\displaystyle(J_{1})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{s_{1}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{k!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[(d_{ij})^{k}f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(0)\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{(s_{1}+1)!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[(d_{ij})^{s_{1}+1}f^{(s_{1}+1)}_{(ij)}(\tilde{d}_{ij})\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big]=\sum_{k=0}^{s}(J_{1})_{ij,k}+\mathsf{Rem}.

where
\tilde{d}_{ij}\in[0,d_{ij}]
. Before proceeding to the estimation of
(J_{1})_{ij,k}
and
\mathsf{Rem}
, we first establish perturbation bounds for the entries of the resolvents, which are useful for the estimation of
f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(0)
and
f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(\tilde{d}_{ij})
.
Using Lemma
5.2
and the notation therein, we have for any
\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{v}\in[M+N]
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\big[R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}},z)-R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0},z)\big]_{\mathfrak{u}\mathfrak{v}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\big[\big(R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}},z)\mathcal{L}(z^{1/2}d_{ij}\mathsf{E}_{ij})\big)^{j}R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}},z)\big]_{\mathfrak{u}\mathfrak{v}}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\big[\big(R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}},z)\mathcal{L}(z^{1/2}d_{ij}\mathsf{E}_{ij})\big)^{s+1}R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0},z)\big]_{\mathfrak{u}\mathfrak{v}}.

Further using the fact that
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}|d_{ij}|\leq N^{-\epsilon_{b}}
,
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}|\big[R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}},z)\big]_{\mathfrak{u}\mathfrak{v}}|\leq N^{\varepsilon}/t^{2}
, and the crude bound
\|R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0},z)\|\leq N
when
\mathrm{Im}\,z\geq N^{-1}
, we may choose
s
large enough to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\big|\big[R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}},z)-R(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0},z)\big]_{\mathfrak{u}\mathfrak{v}}\big|\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{s}\Big(\frac{N^{2\varepsilon}}{t^{4}N^{\epsilon_{b}}}\Big)^{j}+\Big(\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{t^{2}N^{\epsilon_{b}}}\Big)^{s+1}N\lesssim 1,

(5.11)

which yields directly a control of
G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0}
,
G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0}
, and
(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0})^{\top}G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0}
on the event
\Omega
. Here we used the fact that
Y^{\top}GY
can be written in terms of
\mathcal{G}
, which can be seen easily by singular value decomposition. Similar estimates hold if
Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,0}
is replaced by
Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\tilde{d}_{ij}}
, we omit repetitive details. By taking derivatives repeatedly similar to (
5.9
) and (
5.10
), it can be easily seen that for any integer
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\lesssim\frac{N^{(C_{0}+2k+2)\varepsilon}}{t^{2k+2}}.

(5.12)

Combining the above estimate with the perturbation bounds in (
5.11
), we have for any
x\in[0,d_{ij}]
,

\displaystyle f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(x)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\lesssim\frac{N^{(C_{0}+2k+2)\varepsilon}}{t^{2k+2}}.

(5.13)

Now we may start to estimate
(J_{1})_{ij,k}
and
\mathsf{Rem}
. Using the above perturbation bounds on the event
\Omega
, we have that there exists some large
K_{2}>0
, such that

\displaystyle|\mathsf{Rem}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{(s_{1}+1)!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\Big|(d_{ij})^{s_{1}+1}f^{(s_{1}+1)}_{(ij)}(\tilde{d}_{ij})\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{(s_{1}+1)!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\Big|(d_{ij})^{s_{1}+1}f^{(s_{1}+1)}_{(ij)}(\tilde{d}_{ij})\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}w_{ij}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)\Big|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{c}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{(C_{0}+2s_{1}+4)\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{t^{2s_{1}+4}N^{1/2+\epsilon_{a}+(s_{1}+1)\epsilon_{b}}}+N^{K_{2}}Q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

(5.14)

Therefore, with the fact that
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{b}/8}
, we may choose
\varepsilon<\epsilon_{b}/8
and
s_{1}>C_{0}/4+6/\epsilon_{b}
to obtain that

\displaystyle|\mathsf{Rem}|\lesssim N^{-3}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+N^{K_{2}}Q_{0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

We estimate
(J_{1})_{ij,k}
for different
k
separately. For the case when
k
is even, it follows from the symmetric condition that
(J_{1})_{ij,k}=0
. Thus we mainly focus on the estimation for
k
is odd.

Case 1:
k\geq 5
.
First note by symmetry condition, we can obtain

\displaystyle|(J_{1})_{ij,k}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}+u_{2}\geq 1,u_{3}\geq 0\\
u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3}=(k+1)/2\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|\mathsf{A}_{ij}|^{2u_{1}}|t^{1/2}w_{ij}|^{2u_{2}}|b_{ij}|^{2u_{3}}|f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(0)|\Big]\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\lesssim\frac{t\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[|f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(0)|]}{N^{2+(k-3)\epsilon_{b}}},

where in the last step we also used the fact that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}(b_{ij}^{k})\leq N^{-\varepsilon_{b}(k-2)}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}(x_{ij}^{2})\lesssim N^{-1-\varepsilon_{b}(k-2)}
for
k\geq 2
.
We need to estimate
f^{(k)}_{(ij)}(0)
again by Taylor expansion. For an
s_{2}
to be chosen later, there exists
\hat{d}_{ij}\in[0,d_{ij}]
such that

\displaystyle|(J_{1})_{ij,k}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell=0}^{s_{2}}\frac{t\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[|f^{(k+\ell)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})|]}{N^{2+(k+\ell-3)\epsilon_{b}}}+\frac{t\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[|f^{(k+s_{2}+1)}_{(ij)}(\hat{d}_{ij})|]}{N^{2+(k+s_{2}-2)\epsilon_{b}}}.

(5.15)

On the event
\Omega^{c}
, we may estimate the RHS in the above display as in the last step in (
5.14
), which gives

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{\ell=0}^{s_{2}}\frac{t{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[|f^{(k+\ell)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{c}}]}{N^{2+(k+\ell-3)\epsilon_{b}}}+\frac{t{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[|f^{(k+s_{2}+1)}_{(ij)}(\hat{d}_{ij})|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{c}}]}{N^{2+(k+s_{2}-2)\epsilon_{b}}}\Big)\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\lesssim N^{K_{3}}Q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0},

(5.16)

for some large
K_{3}>0
. On the event
\Omega
, we may choose
s_{2}>C_{0}+30+4/\epsilon_{b}
and
\varepsilon<\epsilon_{b}/8
to obtain that

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{\ell=0}^{s_{2}}\frac{t}{N^{2+(k+\ell-3)\epsilon_{b}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|f^{(k+\ell)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\Big]+\frac{t}{N^{2+(k+s_{2}-2)\epsilon_{b}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|f^{(k+s_{2}+1)}_{(ij)}(\hat{d}_{ij})|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\Big]\Big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell=0}^{s_{2}}\frac{t\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{N^{2+(k+\ell-3)\epsilon_{b}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|f^{(k+\ell)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\Big]+N^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell=0}^{s_{2}}\frac{N^{2(k+\ell+1)\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{N^{2+(k+\ell-3)\epsilon_{b}}t^{2(k+\ell)+1}}\sum_{m=1}^{k+\ell+1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(m)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]+N^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

(5.17)

Collecting the above estimates and choosing
\varepsilon<\epsilon_{b}/100
, we have

\displaystyle|(J_{1})_{ij,k}|\lesssim\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{N^{2+\epsilon_{b}/2}}\sum_{m=1}^{k+s_{2}+1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(m)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{N^{2+\epsilon_{b}/2}}+N^{K_{3}}Q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

Case 2:
k=3
.
By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle(J_{1})_{ij,3}\asymp{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\Big(\mathsf{A}_{ij}^{4}+t\mathsf{A}_{ij}^{2}w_{ij}^{2}+t^{2}w_{ij}^{4}+\mathsf{A}_{ij}^{2}\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{2}+tw_{ij}^{2}\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{2}\Big)f^{(3)}_{(ij)}(0)\Big]\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

The term
\mathsf{A}_{ij}^{2}\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{2}
becomes null due to the definitions of
\mathsf{A}_{ij}
and
\mathsf{B}_{ij}
. Concerning the remaining terms, we only show how to estimate the term involving
tw_{ij}^{2}\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{2}
while the others can be handled similarly. Applying Taylor expansion, and then estimating terms on
\Omega^{c}
and
\Omega
separaterly as in (
5.15
)-(
5.17
), we have for
s_{3}>C_{0}/4+\epsilon_{b}/2+4
,

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[tw_{ij}^{2}\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{2}f^{(3)}_{(ij)}(0)\Big]\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell=0}^{s_{3}}\frac{t\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[|f^{(3+\ell)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}]}{N^{2+\ell\epsilon_{b}}}+N^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+N^{K_{5}}Q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0},

for some large
K_{5}>0
. Then it remains to estimate the first term of the RHS of the above inequality. For
\ell\geq 1
, the estimate is similar to (
5.17
), we omit further details. Here we focus on the non-trivial term when
\ell=0
. It is straightforward to compute that
f^{(3)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})
is the products of
F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big),\ell\in[4]
, and the entries of
G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}
,
G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}
, and
(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}})^{\top}G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}
, where the entries’ indices can be
(i,i),(j,j),(i,j),(a,i),(a,j)
,
(i,b),(j,b)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{t}{N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|f^{(3)}_{(ij)}(d_{ij})|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{tN^{6\varepsilon}}{N^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{N^{6\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t^{7}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot(1-\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}}).

This eventually leads to

\displaystyle|(J_{1})_{ij,3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{tN^{6\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}}}{N^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{N^{6\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}(1-\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}})}{N^{2}t^{7}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{N^{2+\epsilon_{b}/2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{s_{3}+4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]+N^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+N^{K_{5}}Q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

The second term in the above display can be estimated by the fact that
|\mathcal{D}_{r}|\vee|\mathcal{D}_{c}|\leq N^{1-\epsilon_{d}}
for some
\epsilon_{d}>0
. By the fact that
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{d}/20}\vee N^{-\epsilon_{b}/20}
, we then have

\displaystyle|(J_{1})_{ij,3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{t^{1/2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot(1-\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}})}{N^{2-\epsilon_{d}/2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{N^{2+\epsilon_{b}/2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{s_{3}+4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]+N^{-3}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+N^{K_{5}}Q_{0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

Case 3:
k=1
.
In this case, using the fact that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[b_{ij}]=0
, we may compute

\displaystyle(J_{1})_{ij,1}=\gamma{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\Big((1-\chi_{ij})^{2}a^{2}_{ij}-tw^{2}_{ij}\Big)f^{(1)}_{(ij)}(0)\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

Recall that
t=N{\mathbb{E}}(\mathsf{A}_{ij}^{2})=N{\mathbb{E}}\big((1-\psi_{ij})^{2}(1-\chi_{ij})^{2}a_{ij}^{2}\big)
. This gives

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbb{E}}\big((1-\chi_{ij})^{2}a_{ij}^{2}\big)-{\mathbb{E}}\big(tw_{ij}^{2}\big)\big|\lesssim tN^{-1-\alpha/2+\alpha\epsilon_{b}}.

(5.18)

Therefore, following the same procedure as in (
5.15
)-(
5.17
), we can also obtain that for sufficiently large constant
s_{4}
,

\displaystyle|(J_{1})_{ij,1}|\lesssim\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{N^{2+\epsilon_{b}/2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{s_{4}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|F^{(\ell)}\big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G_{(ij)}^{\gamma,d_{ij}}\big]_{ab}\big)|\Big]+N^{-3}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+N^{K_{5}}Q_{0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

By combining the estimates of
(J_{1})_{ij,k}
’s with
(J_{2})_{ij}
’s, we can conclude that (
5.8
) holds when we choose
\varepsilon\leq\min\{\epsilon_{\alpha},\epsilon_{b},\epsilon_{d}\}/(100)
.
∎

5.4.
Proof of Theorem
4.3

Since we need to perform the comparison at a random edge, we begin with some preliminary estimates for the derivatives w.r.t. the matrix entries of the random edge.

Lemma 5.3

(
[
23
]
, Lemma 5)
.

Denote
a_{k,\pm}(t)=\Phi_{t}(\zeta_{k,\pm}(t))
,
1\leq k\leq q
. Then
(a_{k,\pm}(t),\zeta_{k,\pm}(t))
are real solutions of

\displaystyle F_{t}(z,\zeta)=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\partial F_{t}}{\partial\zeta}(z,\zeta)=0,

where

\displaystyle F_{t}(z,\zeta)=1+\frac{t(1-c_{N})-\sqrt{t^{2}(1-c_{N})^{2}+4\zeta z}}{2\zeta}-c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta).

Using the lemma above, we can derive bounds for the derivatives of the random edge
\lambda_{-,t}
w.r.t. the matrix entries
b_{ij}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Suppose that
\Psi
is good. If we view
\lambda_{-,t}
as a function of
\mathsf{B}_{ij},i\in[M],j\in[N]
. For any
i\in[M]
and
j\in[N]
, write
\lambda_{-,t}(x)=\lambda_{-,t}(\mathsf{B}_{ij}=x)
. Then for any integer
k\geq 1
and for any
b\in[0,\mathsf{B}_{ij}]
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial^{k}\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{k}}(b)\Big|\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\prec\frac{1}{Nt^{2k+1}},\qquad\Big|\frac{\partial^{k}\zeta_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{k}}(b)\Big|\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\prec\frac{1}{Nt^{2k+1}}.

(5.19)

Further, there exists some constants
C_{k}>0
such that the following deterministic bounds hold,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}}(b)\Big|\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\leq N^{C_{1}},\qquad\Big|\frac{\partial^{k}\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{k}}(b)\Big|\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot\Xi(\lambda_{-,t})\leq N^{C_{k}},

(5.20)

where
\Xi(x)
is a smooth cut off function which equals
0
when
x<\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}/100
and
1
when
x>\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}/2
and
|\Xi^{(n)}(x)|=\mathcal{O}(1)
for all
n\geq 1
.

Remark 7
.

Here we remark that in the second estimate of (
5.20
), we added a cutoff function, in order to get a deterministic bound for the
\lambda_{-,t}
derivatives, which is needed when we take expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\Psi}
. Hence, actually, we should work with
{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(|N\eta\big(\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z_{t})-\mathrm{Im}\,\tilde{m}^{0}(z_{t})\big)\Xi(\lambda_{-,t})|^{2p}\big)
instead of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(|N\eta\big(\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z_{t})-\mathrm{Im}\,\tilde{m}^{0}(z_{t})\big)|^{2p}\big)
to make sure that all quantities in the expansions have bounded expectations. Adding such a cutoff factor will not complicates the expansions since again by the chain rule it boils down to the
\lambda_{-,t}
derivatives. Hence, additional technical inputs are not needed for the comparison of the modified quantity. However, in order to ease the presentation, we will state the reasoning for the original quantity and proceed as if all random factors in the expansion have deterministic upper bound.

Proof.

Let
\psi_{ij}=0
.
To emphasis the dependence with
X
, we first note that
F_{t}(z,\zeta)
can be rewritten as,

\displaystyle F_{t}(z,\zeta,X)=1+\frac{t(1-c_{N})-\sqrt{t^{2}(1-c_{N})^{2}+4\zeta z}}{2\zeta}-\frac{c_{N}t}{M}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(X,\zeta).

Using Lemma
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle F_{t}(\lambda_{-,t},\zeta_{-,t},X)=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\partial F_{t}}{\partial\zeta}(\lambda_{-,t},\zeta_{-,t},X)=0.

(5.21)

Then taking derivative of (
5.21
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}}\frac{\partial F_{t}}{\partial z}(\lambda_{-,t},\zeta_{-,t},X)+\frac{\partial F_{t}}{\partial x_{ij}}(\lambda_{-,t},\zeta_{-,t},X))=0.

Therefore, we may solve the above equation to obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}}=\frac{2c_{N}t\sqrt{t^{2}(1-c_{N})^{2}+4\lambda_{-,t}\zeta_{-,t}}}{M}\big[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\big]_{ji}.

(5.22)

Notice that

\displaystyle\big|\big[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\big]_{ji}\big|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}\big|\big[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}X\big]_{jj}\big|^{1/2}\cdot\big|\big[(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\big]_{ii}\big|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\big|\big[G(X^{\top},\zeta_{-,t})\big]_{jj}+\zeta_{-,t}\big[(G(X^{\top},\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\big]_{jj}\big|^{1/2}\cdot\big|\big[(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\big]_{ii}\big|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|G(X^{\top},\zeta_{-,t})\|^{1/2}+|\zeta_{-,t}|\|G(X^{\top},\zeta_{-,t})\|\big)\cdot\|G(X,\zeta_{-,t})\|\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\prec}t^{-4},

(5.23)

where in
(\mathrm{i})
we applied Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and in
(\mathrm{ii})
we used Lemma
3.2
(i).
Therefore, we can obtain that
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}\lambda_{-,t}(b)
.

Next we view
\Phi_{t}(\zeta)
as a function of
X
, and write
\Phi_{t}(\zeta,X)=\Phi_{t}(\zeta)
. By Lemma
3.1
, we have

\frac{\partial\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta}(\zeta_{-,t},X)=0.

Further taking derivative w.r.t
\mathsf{B}_{ij}
on this equation gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta^{2}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)\frac{\partial\zeta_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta\partial x_{ij}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)=0.

(5.24)

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta\partial x_{ij}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)=\frac{4c_{N}t}{M}[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}-\frac{2c_{N}^{2}t^{2}m_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})}{M}[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{8c_{N}t\zeta_{-,t}(1-c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t}))}{M}[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{3}]_{ji}-\frac{4c_{N}^{2}t^{2}\zeta_{-,t}m_{X}^{\prime}(\zeta_{-,t})}{M}[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4c_{N}(1-c_{N})t^{2}}{M}[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}.

A similar argument as in (
5.23
) leads to
\big[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{3}\big]_{ji}\prec t^{-6}
. This together with the fact that

c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})\prec t^{1/2}
and
m_{X}^{\prime}(\zeta_{-,t})\sim t^{-1}
gives

\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta\partial x_{ij}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)\prec{1}/{(Nt^{5})}.

We can also compute that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta^{2}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)

\displaystyle=-2c_{N}tm_{X}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta_{-,t})\zeta_{-,t}(1-c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t}))-4c_{N}tm_{X}^{\prime}(\zeta_{-,t})(1-c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t}))

\displaystyle\quad+2\zeta_{-,t}(c_{N}tm_{X}^{\prime}(\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}-c_{N}(1-c_{N})t^{2}m_{X}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta_{-,t}).

(5.25)

Using Lemma
2.3
with the fact
\zeta_{-,t}-\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))\sim t^{2}
w.h.p., we have w.h.p. that

\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta^{2}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)\sim t^{2}.

Combining the above bounds gives

\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}\zeta_{-,t}\prec{1}/{(Nt^{3})}.

It is worth noting that for any integer
k\geq 2
, the
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{k}\lambda_{-,t}
can be expressed as a function of
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{\ell}\lambda_{-,t}
and
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{\ell}\zeta_{-,t}
, where
\ell
ranges from
0
to
k-1
. Similarly,
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{k}\zeta_{-,t}
is solely dependent on
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{\ell}\zeta_{-,t}
, where
\ell
ranges from
0
to
k-1
. By employing the product rule and adopting a similar argument as used in (
5.23
) to bound the Green function entries, we can observe that the order of
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{\ell}\lambda_{-,t}
is determined by the term that includes
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{k-1}[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}
. Similarly, the order of
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{\ell}\zeta_{-,t}
is determined by the term that includes
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}^{k-1}[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{3}]_{ji}
. This allows us to conclude that for any
k\geq 1

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{k}\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{k}}\prec\frac{1}{Nt^{2k+1}},\qquad\frac{\partial^{k}\zeta_{-,t}}{\partial\mathsf{B}_{ij}^{k}}\prec\frac{1}{Nt^{2k+1}}.

The claim now follows by noting that the above bounds still hold when we replace
\mathsf{B}_{ij}
in
X
with some other
b\in[0,\mathsf{B}_{ij}]
. The reason behind this is that the replacement matrix still satisfies the
\eta^{*}
-regularity condition, ensuring that the corresponding
\zeta_{-,t}
and
\lambda_{M}
still satisfy Lemma
3.2
(i).

Next, we prove a deterministic upper bound for
\partial_{\mathsf{B}_{ij}}\lambda_{-,t}
. For notational simplicity, we will only work on the original matrix
X
, and the argument holds for the replacement matrix
X_{(ij)}(b)
. In view of (
5.22
), it suffices to obtain deterministic upper bounds for
\lambda_{-,t}
,
\zeta_{-,t}
, and
[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}
. We may first apply Cauchy interlacing theorem to obtain an upper bound for
\zeta_{-,t}
as follows:

\displaystyle\zeta_{-,t}\leq\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))\leq\lambda_{M-|\mathcal{D}_{r}|}(\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{B}^{(\mathcal{D}_{r})}))\leq N^{2-2\epsilon_{b}},

(5.26)

where in the last step we used the fact that the entries of
\mathcal{S}(\mathsf{B}^{(\mathcal{D}_{r})})
are bounded by
N^{-\epsilon_{b}}
. From (
2.2
), we have

c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})={c_{N}tm_{t}(\lambda_{-,t})}/{(1+c_{N}tm_{t}(\lambda_{-,t}))},

which gives the deterministic bound
m_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})\leq(c_{N}t)^{-1}
. Using this deterministic bound, we have that there exists some constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}\leq\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\frac{\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))-\zeta_{-,t}}{\lambda_{i}(\mathcal{S}(X))-\zeta_{-,t}}\leq Ct^{-1}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))-\zeta_{-,t}).

This together with the fact that
\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))\geq\zeta_{-,t}
(cf. Lemma
3.2
(i)) gives
\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))-\zeta_{-,t}\geq C^{-1}t/M
.
Therefore, we are able to obtain deterministic bounds for the high order derivatives
m_{X}^{(k)}(\zeta_{-,t})
as well as the spectral norm of
G(X,\zeta_{-,t})
.
We can also obtain that

\displaystyle|\lambda_{-,t}|=\big|\big[1-c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})\big]^{2}\zeta_{-,t}+(1-c_{N})t\big[1-c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})\big]\big|\lesssim N^{2-2\epsilon_{b}}.

For the upper bound of
|[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}|
, we have

\displaystyle|[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}]_{ji}|

\displaystyle\leq\big|[X^{\top}(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}X]_{jj}\big|^{1/2}\cdot\big|[(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\big]_{ii}|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}XX^{\top}\|^{1/2}\cdot\|(G(X,\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\|G(X,\zeta_{-,t})\|^{1/2}+|\zeta_{-,t}|\|G(X,\zeta_{-,t})\|\big)\cdot\|G(X,\zeta_{-,t})\|\lesssim N^{2-2\epsilon_{b}}M^{2}t^{-2}.

Collecting the above bounds proves the first bound in (
5.20
).

To prove the second bound in (
5.20
), it suffices to provide a lower bound for
\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta^{2}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)
(cf. (
5.24
)). When
\lambda_{-,t}\geq\lambda_{-}^{\mathsf{mp}}/100
, we have

\displaystyle|c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})|=\Big|\frac{c_{N}tm_{t}(\lambda_{-,t})}{1+c_{N}tm_{t}(\lambda_{-,t})}\Big|\leq|c_{N}tm_{t}(\lambda_{-,t})|\lesssim\frac{t^{1/2}}{|\lambda_{-t}|}\lesssim t^{1/2}.

Therefore, using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle(c_{N}tm_{X}^{\prime}(\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}\leq\frac{c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})\cdot c_{N}tm^{\prime\prime}_{X}(\zeta_{-,t})}{2}\ll c_{N}tm^{\prime\prime}_{X}(\zeta_{-,t}).

This implies that

-2c_{N}tm_{X}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta_{-,t})\zeta_{-,t}(1-c_{N}tm_{X}(\zeta_{-,t}))+2\zeta_{-,t}(c_{N}tm_{X}^{\prime}(\zeta_{-,t}))^{2}<0.

Then using (
5.25
), we may lower bound
\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta^{2}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)
as follows:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi_{t}}{\partial\zeta^{2}}(\zeta_{-,t},X)\Big|>c_{N}(1-c_{N})t^{2}m_{X}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta_{-,t})\geq\frac{2c_{N}(1-c_{N})t^{2}}{M(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))-\zeta_{-,t})^{3}}\geq\frac{2c_{N}(1-c_{N})t^{2}}{MN^{6-6\epsilon_{b}}},

where in the last step we used (
5.26
).
∎

Next, we start the proof of Theorem
4.3
.

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.

We begin by collecting some notation to simplify the presentation of the proof. Consider
\tilde{w}_{ij}
as the
(i,j)
-entry of
\tilde{W}
, and define
\tilde{Y}^{\gamma}
analogously to
Y^{\gamma}
, with the substitution of
W
by
\tilde{W}
. Recall (
5.2
) and we write
d_{ij}=d_{ij}(\gamma,w_{ij})
,
e_{ij}=e_{ij}(\gamma,w_{ij})
,
\tilde{d}_{ij}=d_{ij}(0,\tilde{w}_{ij})
, and
\tilde{e}_{ij}=\tilde{e}_{ij}(0,\tilde{w}_{ij})
in the sequel. To emphasize that
\lambda_{-,t}
is a function of
X
, we introduce the notation
\lambda^{(ij)}_{-,t}(\beta)=\lambda_{-,t}(X_{(ij)}^{\beta})
. Consequently, we define
z^{(ij)}_{t}(\beta)=\lambda_{-,t}^{(ij)}(\beta)+E+\mathrm{i}\eta
. For simplicity, we use the shorthand notation
G^{\gamma,\lambda,\beta}_{(ij)}
as
G^{\gamma,\lambda}_{(ij)}\big(z_{t}^{(ij)}(\beta)\big)
, and we define
\tilde{G}^{\gamma,\lambda,\beta}_{(ij)}
analogously, replacing
W
with
\tilde{W}
.

We will focus on the estimation of
\frac{\partial{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}(|N\eta(\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z_{t})-\mathrm{Im}\,\tilde{m}^{0}(z_{t}))|^{2p})}{\partial\gamma}
. To this end, let us define
f_{\gamma,(ab),(ij)}(\lambda,\beta)=\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma,\lambda,\beta}_{(ij)}]_{ab}
,
\tilde{f}_{\gamma,(ab),(ij)}(\lambda,\beta)=\mathrm{Im}\,[\tilde{G}^{\gamma,\lambda,\beta}_{(ij)}]_{ab}
,
g_{(ij)}(\lambda,\beta)=\eta\mathrm{Im}\,[\big(G^{\gamma,\lambda,\beta}_{(ij)}\big)^{2}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,\lambda,\beta}]_{ij}
, and
F_{p}(\lambda,\tilde{\lambda},\beta)=(\eta\sum_{a}f_{\gamma,(aa),(ij)}(\lambda,\beta)-\eta\sum_{a}\tilde{f}_{0,(aa),(ij)}(\tilde{\lambda}_{,}\beta))^{p}
. Some elementary calculation gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|N\eta\big(\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z_{t})-\mathrm{Im}\,\tilde{m}^{0}(z_{t})\big)\big|^{2p}\big)}{\partial\gamma}=-2p\sum_{i,j}\Big((J_{1})_{ij}+(J_{2})_{ij}\Big),

where

\displaystyle(J_{1})_{ij}={\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[g_{(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\mathcal{E}_{ij}F_{2p-1}(d_{ij},\tilde{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0},

\displaystyle(J_{2})_{ij}=-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[w_{ij}g_{(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})F_{2p-1}(e_{ij},\tilde{e}_{ij},c_{ij})\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{ij}=(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}-\gamma t^{1/2}(1-\gamma^{2})^{-1/2}w_{ij}.

For
(J_{2})_{ij}
, we may apply Gaussian integration by parts to obtain that

\displaystyle(J_{2})_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}N}\Big({\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\partial_{w_{ij}}\big\{g_{(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})\big\}F_{2p-1}(e_{ij},\tilde{e}_{ij},c_{ij})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+(2p-1){\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[g_{(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})\partial_{w_{ij}}\big\{\eta\sum_{a}f_{\gamma,(aa),(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})\big\}F_{2p-2}(e_{ij},\tilde{e}_{ij},c_{ij})\Big]\Big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}.

Note by directly calculation, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{w_{ij}}\big\{g_{(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})\big\}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathrm{Im}\,\big[\big(G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big)^{2}\big]_{ii}-2\mathrm{Im}\,\big(\big[\big(G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big)^{2}\big]_{ii}\big[(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}})^{\top}G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{jj}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-2\mathrm{Im}\,\big(\big[\big(G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big)^{2}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ij}\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ij}\big)\Big)(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}t^{1/2}\eta,

(5.27)

and
\partial_{w_{ij}}\big\{\eta\sum_{a}f_{\gamma,(aa),(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})\big\}=-2t^{1/2}(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}g_{(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})
. Using Wald’s identity with the fact that
i\in\mathcal{D}_{r}
and
j\in\mathcal{D}_{c}
when
\psi_{ij}=1
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle|\big[\big(G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big)^{2}\big]_{ii}|\leq\sum_{a}|\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big]_{ai}|^{2}=\frac{\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big]_{ii}}{\eta}\prec t^{-2}\eta^{-1},

(5.28)

and

\displaystyle|\big[(G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)})^{2}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{ij}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a}|\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{aj}|^{2}+\sum_{a}|\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big]_{ia}|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\big[(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}})^{\top}|G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}|^{2}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}\big]_{jj}+\eta^{-1}\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big]_{ii}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{=}\big[(Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}})^{\top}Y_{(ij)}^{\gamma,e_{ij}}|\mathcal{G}^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}|^{2}\big]_{jj}+\eta^{-1}\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big]_{ii}

\displaystyle=\big[\bar{\mathcal{G}}^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big]_{jj}+z\big[|\mathcal{G}^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}|^{2}\big]_{jj}+\eta^{-1}\mathrm{Im}\,\big[G^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}\big]_{ii}\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\prec}t^{-2}\eta^{-1},

(5.29)

where in
(\mathrm{i})
we applied Wald’s identity, in
(\mathrm{ii})
we used the fact that for any
A\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
,
(AA^{\top}-z)^{-1}A=A^{\top}(AA^{\top}-z)^{-1}
when
z
does not lie inside the spectrum of
A
, and in
(\mathrm{iii})
we estimate
\big[|\mathcal{G}^{\gamma,e_{ij},c_{ij}}_{(ij)}|^{2}\big]_{jj}
in a similar way as done in (
5.28
).

Combining the above estimates with the fact that
\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}\leq N^{1-\epsilon_{\alpha}}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle|(J_{2})_{ij}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}N^{1-\epsilon_{\alpha}}}\sum_{k=1}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[|\mathcal{O}_{\prec}(N^{-\epsilon_{\alpha}}t^{-3})|\cdot|F_{2p-k}(e_{ij},\tilde{e}_{ij},c_{ij})|\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}.
