t\gg N^{-2/7+\alpha/14}
.

To evaluate
|T_{2}|
, we start by expressing it as follows:

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big({\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\varepsilon_{k}){\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\log|1+N^{-1}\sigma_{N}^{2}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(\tilde{X}^{(k)},z^{\prime})+\mathcal{E}|^{2})\big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big({\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\varepsilon_{k}){\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\log|1+N^{-1}\sigma_{N}^{2}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(\tilde{X}^{(k)},z^{\prime})|^{2})\big)+{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big({\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\varepsilon_{k}){\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\log|1+\mathcal{E}J_{k,{\mathrm{Tr}}}|^{2})\big).

First, we use the fact that
\log|1+N^{-1}\sigma_{N}^{2}{\mathrm{Tr}}G(\tilde{X}^{(k)},z)|^{2}
is independent of
\tilde{x}_{k}
and that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k})=0
to obtain the inequality

T_{2}\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big(|{\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\varepsilon_{k})|\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{k}|\mathcal{E}|\big).

Next, we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to obtain

T_{2}\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big(|{\mathbb{E}}_{k}(\varepsilon_{k})|^{2}\big){\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big(|{\mathbb{E}}_{k}(|\mathcal{E}|)|^{2}\big)}.

Finally, by Jensen’s inequality, we have

T_{2}\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}_{k}{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big(|\varepsilon_{k}|^{2}\big){\mathbb{E}}_{k}{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\big(|\mathcal{E}|^{2}\big)}\leq N^{1-\alpha/2}\cdot N^{2/\alpha+3\alpha/8+\epsilon_{\vartheta}(4-\alpha)/2-2}t^{-9/2}.

The bound
|T_{2}|\ll t^{2}N^{1-\alpha/2}
follows by the facts
\epsilon_{\vartheta}<(3\alpha-5)/(4\alpha)
and
t\gg N^{(\alpha-4)/56}
. The same bound holds for
|T_{1}|
. Therefore, we can obtain that for any
z\in\omega\cap\gamma
and
z^{\prime}\in\omega^{\prime}\cap\gamma
,
|\varphi_{k}-\tilde{\varphi}_{k}|\ll t^{2}N^{1-\alpha/2}
, which conludes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.13

(
[
16
]
, Lemma 4.1)
.

Let
a\equiv(a_{1},\cdots,a_{N})^{\top}
be a column vector whose entries are i.i.d. centered and satisfy
({ii})
and
({iii})
in Lemma
3.12
. Then for deterministic matrix
G
, the random variables

\displaystyle X\equiv\sum_{i\neq j}G_{ij}a_{i}a_{j},\quad E\equiv\sum_{i}G_{ii}a_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{N}{\mathrm{Tr}}G

satisfy

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}|X|^{2}\leq 2N^{-1}\|G\|^{2},\quad{\mathbb{E}}|E|^{2}\leq 10C(\|G\|^{2}+1)N^{\vartheta(4-\alpha)-1}.

The following lemma is a directly consequence of Lemma
A.13
.

Lemma A.14
.

Fix
C>0
. For any
\zeta\in\{\xi\in\mathbb{C}:|\xi-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}|\leq Ct^{2}\}
, we have there exist constant
\tau=\tau(C)
such that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{x}_{k}}(|\eta_{k}|^{2})\leq\tau^{-2}N^{-1}t^{-4}
on the event
\Omega_{k}=\{\lambda_{1}(\mathcal{S}(\tilde{X}^{(k)}))-\bar{\zeta}_{-,t}\geq\tau t^{2}\}
.

Appendix B
Remaining proofs for the general model

B.1.
Proof of Lemma
5.1

We need the following lemma on the monotonicity of the Green function to the linearization of
\mathcal{S}(Y^{\gamma})
.

Lemma B.1

(
[
11
]
, Lemma 2.1)
.

For deterministic matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times N}
, let
\mathcal{L}(A)
be defined as in Eq. (
5.2
) Further define
\Gamma(z)\coloneqq\max_{i,j\in[M+N]}[(\mathcal{L}(A)-z)^{-1}]_{ij}\vee 1
.
We have for any
L>1
and
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, we have
\Gamma(E+\mathrm{i}\eta/L)\leq L\Gamma(E+\mathrm{i}\eta)
.

Recall that for any
\delta>0
,
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathsf{D}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{0}(\delta,z,\Psi)=\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in[M]\\
0\leq\gamma\leq 1\end{subarray}}|z^{1/2}\mathfrak{X}_{ab}[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab}|>N^{\delta}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{1}(\delta,z,\Psi)=\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,v\in[N]\\
0\leq\gamma\leq 1\end{subarray}}|z^{1/2}\mathfrak{Y}_{uv}[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z)]_{uv}|>N^{\delta}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{2}(\delta,z,\Psi)=\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in[M],u\in[N]\\
0\leq\gamma\leq 1\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{Z}_{au}[G^{\gamma}(z)Y^{\gamma}]_{au}|>N^{\delta}\Big).

Now let us give the proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Let
p
be any sufficiently large (but fixed) integer, and
F_{p}(x):=|x|^{2p}+1
. It can be easily verified that there exists a constant
C_{p}
, only depends on
p
such that
|F^{(a)}_{p}(x)|\leq C_{p}F_{p}(x)
, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
. Recall Theorem
4.2
, and we will focus on the case when
(\#_{1},\#_{2},\#_{3})=(\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab},\;\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{0}(z)]_{ab},\;\mathfrak{I}_{0,ab})
therein.
Applying Theorem
4.2
with
F(x)=F_{p}(x)
, we have for any
a,b\in[M]
, there exists constant
C_{1}>0
such that,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F_{p}(\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab})\big)-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F_{p}(\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{0}(z)]_{ab})\big)<C_{1}N^{-\omega}(\mathfrak{I}_{p,0}+1)+C_{1}Q_{0}N^{C_{1}},

where

\mathfrak{I}_{p,0}\equiv\sup_{{i,j\in[M],0\leq\gamma\leq 1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|F_{p}(\mathfrak{X}_{ij}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ij})\big|\big).

Taking supremum over
a,b\in[M]
and
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
yields

\displaystyle(1-C_{1}N^{-\omega})\mathfrak{I}_{p,0}\leq\max_{i,j\in[M]}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F_{p}(\mathfrak{X}_{ij}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{0}(z)]_{ij})\big)+C_{1}N^{-\omega}+3C_{1}N^{C_{1}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon,z,\Psi).

Applying Lemma
B.1
on
\mathcal{R}(Y^{\gamma},z)=z^{-1/2}(\mathcal{L}(Y^{\gamma})-z^{1/2})^{-1}
with
z^{1/2}=\tilde{E}+\mathrm{i}\tilde{\eta}
, we have,

\displaystyle\max_{i,j\in[M+N]}|z^{1/2}[\mathcal{R}(Y^{\gamma},z)]_{ij}|\vee 1\leq L\Big(\max_{i,j\in[M+N]}|(z^{\prime})^{1/2}[\mathcal{R}(Y^{\gamma},z^{\prime})]_{ij}|\vee 1\Big),

for any
L>0
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
satisfies
(z^{\prime})^{1/2}=\tilde{E}+\mathrm{i}L\tilde{\eta}
. Let
L\equiv N^{\varepsilon/6}
and thus
(z^{\prime})^{1/2}\equiv\tilde{E}+\mathrm{i}N^{\varepsilon/6}\tilde{\eta}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\max_{i,j\in[M]}|z^{1/2}[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ij}|\vee 1,\;\max_{i,j\in[N]}|z^{1/2}[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z)]_{ij}|\vee 1,\;\max_{i\in[M],j\in[N]}|[G^{\gamma}(z)Y^{\gamma}]_{ij}|\leq\mathfrak{S},

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}\equiv N^{\varepsilon/6}\Big(\max_{i,j\in[M]}|(z^{\prime})^{1/2}[G^{\gamma}(z^{\prime})]_{ij}|\vee\max_{i,j\in[N]}|(z^{\prime})^{1/2}[\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}(z^{\prime})]_{ij}|\vee\max_{i\in[M],j\in[N]}|[G^{\gamma}(z^{\prime})Y^{\gamma}]_{ij}|\vee 1\Big).

This implies that

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon,z,\Psi)\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z^{\prime},\Psi).

(B.1)

For any
z_{0}=E_{0}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}\in\mathsf{D}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3})
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F_{p}(\mathfrak{X}_{ij}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{0}(z_{0})]_{ij})\big)\lesssim N
(cf. Theorem
2.10
). Then there exists some large constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{p,0}\leq C_{2}N+C_{2}N^{C_{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon,z_{0},\Psi).

Using (
B.1
) by setting
z\equiv z_{0}
, we have for
z_{1}=E_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{1}
where
(E_{1},\eta_{1})
are defined through (
5.3
),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{p,0}\leq C_{2}N+C_{2}N^{C_{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{1},\Psi).

For any
a,b\in[M]
, and
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, applying Markov’s inequality with the fact that
p\delta>D+100
, we have that there exists some large constant
C_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(|z_{0}^{1/2}\mathfrak{X}_{ab}[\mathrm{Im}\,G^{\gamma}(z_{0})]_{ab}|>N^{\delta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|z_{0}|^{p/2}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(\big|F_{p}(\mathfrak{X}_{ab}\mathrm{Im}\,[G^{\gamma}(z_{0})]_{ab})\big|\big)}{N^{p\delta}}\leq\frac{|z_{1}|^{p/2}\mathfrak{I}_{p,0}}{N^{p\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}N^{-D-90}+C_{3}N^{C_{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{1},\Psi),

where in the last step we used the fact that
|z_{0}|
is bounded. Similar bound holds when
\mathrm{Im}\,
is replaced by
\mathrm{Re}\,
, we omit the details.
Now we may apply union bounds on
i,j\in[M]
and an
\epsilon
-net argument on
\gamma
with the following deterministic bounds

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\partial[G^{\gamma}(z)]_{ab}}{\partial\gamma}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\|A\|+\gamma\|t^{1/2}W\|}{\eta^{2}},

\eta>N^{-1}
,
\|A\|\leq N^{1/2}
and
\mathbb{P}(\|t^{1/2}W\|>2)<N^{-D}
, to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{0}(\delta,z_{0},\Psi)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in[M]\\
0\leq\gamma\leq 1\end{subarray}}|z_{0}^{1/2}\mathfrak{X}_{ab}[G^{\gamma}(z_{0})]_{ab}|>N^{\delta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}N^{-D-50}+C_{4}N^{C_{4}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[0:2]\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\varepsilon/2,z_{1},\Psi),

for some large constant
C_{4}>0
. Repeating the above procedure for all
\mathfrak{P}_{k}(\delta,\eta,\Psi),k=1,2
proves the claim.
∎

B.2.
Proof of Corollary
4.6

We prove this corollary using a similar argument as in [Section 4,
[
56
]
] or [Section 4,
[
36
]
]. The key inputs are the rigidity estimate in Theorem
4.4
and the Green function comparison in Theorem
4.5
.

Proof of Corollary
4.6
.

Let us first define for any
E
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(E):=\big|\{i:\lambda_{i}(\mathcal{S}(Y))\leq\lambda_{-,t}+E\}\big|.

For any
\epsilon>0
, we take
\ell=N^{-2/3-\epsilon/3}
and
\eta=N^{-2/3-\epsilon}
. Recall from Theorem
4.4
that
\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))\geq\lambda_{-,t}-N^{-2/3+\epsilon}
holds with high probability. We further define

\displaystyle{\chi}_{E}(x):=\mathbf{1}_{[-N^{-2/3+\epsilon},E]}(x-\lambda_{-,t}),

\displaystyle\theta_{\eta}(x):=\frac{\eta}{\pi(x^{2}+\eta^{2})}=\frac{1}{\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\frac{1}{x-\mathrm{i}\eta}.

Then following the same arguments as in [Lemma 2.7,
[
42
]
], we can obtain that for
|E|\leq N^{-2/3+\epsilon}
, the following holds with high probability:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Tr}}(\chi_{E-\ell}\ast\theta_{\eta})(\mathcal{S}(Y))-N^{-\epsilon/9}\leq\mathcal{N}(E)\leq{\mathrm{Tr}}(\chi_{E+\ell}\ast\theta_{\eta})(\mathcal{S}(Y))+N^{-\epsilon/9}.

Let
K(x):\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
be a smooth monotonic increasing function such that

\displaystyle K(x)=1\quad\text{if}\quad x\geq 2/3,\quad K(x)=0\quad\text{if}\quad x\leq 1/3.

Therefore, we have with high probability that

\displaystyle K({\mathrm{Tr}}(\chi_{E-\ell}\ast\theta_{\eta})(\mathcal{S}(Y)))+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9})

\displaystyle\leq K(\mathcal{N}(E))=\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}(E)\geq 1}

\displaystyle\leq K({\mathrm{Tr}}(\chi_{E+\ell}\ast\theta_{\eta})(\mathcal{S}(Y)))+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9}).

Taking expectation on the above inequality, we have for
|s|\leq N^{\epsilon}/2
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[K\bigg(\mathrm{Im}\,\bigg[\frac{N}{\pi}\int_{-N^{-2/3+\epsilon}}^{sN^{-2/3}-\ell}m^{1}(\lambda_{-,t}+y+\mathrm{i}\eta)\bigg]\mathrm{d}y\bigg)\Bigg]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{-,t})\leq s\Big)={\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{N}(sN^{-2/3})\geq 1}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[K\bigg(\mathrm{Im}\,\bigg[\frac{N}{\pi}\int_{-N^{-2/3+\epsilon}}^{sN^{-2/3}+\ell}m^{1}(\lambda_{-,t}+y+\mathrm{i}\eta)\bigg]\mathrm{d}y\bigg)\Bigg]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9}).

(B.2)

Similarly, repeating the above arguments with
\mathcal{S}(Y)
replaced by
\mathcal{S}(V_{t})
, we can also have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[K\bigg(\mathrm{Im}\,\bigg[\frac{N}{\pi}\int_{-N^{-2/3+\epsilon}}^{sN^{-2/3}-\ell}m^{0}(\lambda_{-,t}+y+\mathrm{i}\eta)\bigg]\mathrm{d}y\bigg)\Bigg]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{-,t})\leq s\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[K\bigg(\mathrm{Im}\,\bigg[\frac{N}{\pi}\int_{-N^{-2/3+\epsilon}}^{sN^{-2/3}+\ell}m^{0}(\lambda_{-,t}+y+\mathrm{i}\eta)\bigg]\mathrm{d}y\bigg)\Bigg]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9}).

(B.3)

Note that the conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}
in (
4.7
) can be replaced by
{\mathbb{E}}
using the law of total expectation together with the fact that
\Omega_{\Psi}
holds with high probability. Therefore, we can combine (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) with (
4.7
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{-,t})\leq s-2\ell N^{-2/3}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9})\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{-,t})\leq s\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(N^{2/3}(\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(V_{t}))-\lambda_{-,t})\leq s+2\ell N^{-2/3}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9}).

Now (
4.8
) follows by the fact that
\ell N^{-2/3}\ll 1
. For (
4.9
), we first note by Theorem
2.14
that

\displaystyle|\lambda_{-,t}-\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}|\leq N^{-2/3+\epsilon}

(B.4)

holds in probability. This together with Theorem
4.4
implies that

\displaystyle|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(Y))-\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}|\leq N^{-2/3+\epsilon}

also holds in probability. Then we may proceed similar to the proof of (
4.8
), but with all high probability estimates replaced by in probability estimates. It’s worth noting that during the derivation of (
B.2
) and (
B.3
), the error term
\mathcal{O}(N^{-\epsilon/9})
will become
\mathfrak{o}(1)
because we lack an polynomial bound for the failure probability of (
B.4
). Finally, we can conclude the proof of (
4.9
) by using Theorem
4.5
.
∎

B.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.5

Proof.

To ease presentation, we show the proof of the following comparison instead: for any
|E|\leq N^{-2/3+\epsilon}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}_{\Psi}\Big(F(N\eta_{0}\mathrm{Im}\,m^{1}(\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}))\Big)-\mathbb{E}_{\Psi}\Big(F(N\eta_{0}\mathrm{Im}\,m^{0}(\lambda_{-,t}+E+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}))\Big)\Big|\leq CN^{-\delta_{1}}.

(B.5)

The proof of (
4.7
) is similar, and thus we omit it. Using the same notation as in the proof of Theorem
4.3
and further defining
h_{\gamma,(ij)}(\lambda,\beta)\equiv\eta_{0}\sum_{a}f_{\gamma,(aa),(ij)}(\lambda,,\beta)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big(F(N\eta_{0}\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z_{t}))\big)}{\partial\gamma}=-2\Big(\sum_{i,j}(I_{1})_{ij}-(I_{2})_{ij}\Big),

with

\displaystyle(I_{1})_{ij}\equiv{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[A_{ij}F^{\prime}\Big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}\big([Y^{\gamma}]_{ij},X_{ij}\big)\Big)g_{(ij)}\big([Y^{\gamma}]_{ij},X_{ij}\big)\bigg],

\displaystyle(I_{2})_{ij}\equiv\frac{\gamma t^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[w_{ij}F^{\prime}\Big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}\big([Y^{\gamma}]_{ij},X_{ij}\big)\Big)g_{(ij)}\big([Y^{\gamma}]_{ij},X_{ij}\big)\bigg].

We first consider the estimation for
(I_{1})_{ij}
. Notice that
(I_{1})_{ij}
can be further decomposed as

\displaystyle(I_{1})_{ij}=(I_{1})_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+(I_{1})_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}=(I_{1})_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0},

where in the last step we used the fact that
A_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}=0
. Therefore, we only need to consider the case when
\psi_{ij}=0
, and
(I_{1})_{ij}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle(I_{1})_{ij}={\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}F^{\prime}\Big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\Big)g_{(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

By Taylor expansion, for an
s_{1}>0
to be chosen later, there exists
\tilde{d}_{ij}\in[0,d_{ij}]
such that,

\displaystyle(I_{1})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{s_{1}}\frac{1}{k_{1}!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{k_{1}}g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(s_{1}+1)!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{s_{1}+1}g_{(ij)}^{(s_{1}+1,0)}(\tilde{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{s_{1}}(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}}+\mathsf{Rem}_{1}.

Using (
5.27
)-(
5.29
), ,the perturbation argument as in (
5.11
), and the fact that
\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z_{t})\prec 1
, we have for any (small)
\epsilon>0
and (large)
D>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\bigg(\Omega_{\epsilon,1}:=\Big\{\big|g_{(ij)}^{(s_{1}+1,0)}(\tilde{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\big|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}<t^{-s_{1}-2}N^{\epsilon}\Big\}\bigg)\geq 1-N^{-D}.

Further, by the Gaussianity of
w_{ij}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Psi}\bigg(\Omega_{\epsilon,2}:=\Big\{\max_{i\in[M],j\in[N]}|t^{1/2}w_{ij}|<N^{-1/2+\epsilon}\Big\}\bigg)\geq 1-N^{-D}.

Let
\Omega_{\epsilon}:=\Omega_{\epsilon,1}\cap\Omega_{\epsilon,2}
. Then

\displaystyle|\mathsf{Rem}_{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[|(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{s_{1}+1}|\cdot\big|g_{(ij)}^{(s_{1}+1,0)}(\tilde{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\big|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\epsilon}}\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[|(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{s_{1}+1}|\cdot\big|g_{(ij)}^{(s_{1}+1,0)}(\tilde{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\big|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{c}_{\epsilon}}\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\lesssim}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[|(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{s_{1}+1}|\cdot\big|g_{(ij)}^{(s_{1}+1,0)}(\tilde{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\big|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\epsilon}}\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\quad+N^{-D+C_{1}+2(s_{1}+3)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\lesssim}\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N^{1/2+\epsilon_{b}(s_{1}+1)}t^{s_{1}+2}},

(B.6)

where in
(\mathrm{i})
we used the deterministic bound
\big|g_{(ij)}^{(s_{1}+1,0)}(\tilde{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\big|\leq N^{C_{1}+2(s_{1}+3)}
when
\eta\geq N^{-2}
, and
(\mathrm{ii})
is a consequence of the definition of
\Omega_{\epsilon}
. Choosing
s_{1}
sufficiently large, i.e.,
s_{1}>4/\epsilon_{b}
, and
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{b}/2}
we can obtain

\displaystyle|\mathsf{Rem}_{1}|\lesssim N^{-5/2}.

For
(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}}
, we need to further expand
F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij})\big)
as follows:

\displaystyle F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)=\sum_{k=0}^{s_{2}}\frac{d_{ij}^{k}}{k!}\frac{\partial^{k}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)+\frac{d_{ij}^{s_{2}+1}}{(s_{2}+1)!}\frac{\partial^{k}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(\hat{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big),

where
s_{2}
is a positive integer to be chosen later, and
\hat{d}_{ij}\in[0,d_{ij}]
. Then
(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}}
can be rewritten as,

\displaystyle(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}}=\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{s_{2}}\frac{1}{k_{1}!k_{2}!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{k_{1}!(s_{2}+1)!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{k_{1}+s_{2}+1}g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\frac{\partial^{s_{2}+1}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{s_{2}+1}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(\hat{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{s_{2}}(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}k_{2}}+\mathsf{Rem}_{2}.

By Faà di Bruno’s formula, we have for any integer
n>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{n}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(m_{1},\cdots,m_{n})}\frac{n!}{m_{1}!m_{2}!\cdot m_{n}!}\cdot F^{(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}+1)}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{h^{(\ell)}_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})}{\ell!}\bigg)^{m_{\ell}}

(B.7)

Considering (
B.7
), (
5.27
)-(
5.29
), and using the perturbation argument as described in (
5.11
), we arrive at the following result:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{s_{2}+1}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{s_{2}+1}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(\hat{d}_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\prec\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}t^{-(\ell+1)m_{\ell}}\leq t^{-2n}.

(B.8)

Moreover, taking into account the fact that
g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1})}(0)\prec t^{-(k_{1}+1)}
, we can deduce that:

\displaystyle|\mathsf{Rem}_{2}|\lesssim\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N^{1/2+\epsilon_{b}(k_{1}+s_{2}+1)}t^{k_{1}+2(s_{2}+1)}}\lesssim N^{-5/2},

where, for the final step, we have chosen
s_{2}\geq 4/\epsilon_{b}
and
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{b}/4}
.
Next, we estimate
(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}k_{2}}
in different cases.

Case 1:

k_{1}+k_{2}
is even. By the law of total expectation,

\displaystyle(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}k_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{k_{1}!k_{2}!}\sum_{n=0}^{1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[(1-\chi_{ij})a_{ij}d_{ij}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg|\chi_{ij}=n\bigg]\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=n)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}}{k_{1}!k_{2}!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[a_{ij}d_{ij}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,0)\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,0)\big)\bigg|\chi_{ij}=0\bigg]\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=0)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}=0}{k_{1}!k_{2}!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[a_{ij}d_{ij}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\bigg|\chi_{ij}=0\bigg]{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,0)\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,0)\big)\bigg]\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=0),

(B.9)

where the last step follows from the symmetry condition.

Case 2:

k_{1}+k_{2}
is odd and
k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 5
. Similar to (
B.9
), we have

\displaystyle|(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}k_{2}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg|{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[a_{ij}d_{ij}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}\bigg|\chi_{ij}=0\bigg]\bigg|{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[|g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,0)|\bigg|\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,0)\big)\bigg|\bigg|\chi_{ij}=0\bigg]\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=0)\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2+2\epsilon_{a}+(k_{1}+k_{2}-3)\epsilon_{b}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[|g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})|\bigg|\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg|\bigg]\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0,\chi_{ij}=0}.

We may again obtain the bound
|g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1})}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0,\chi_{ij}=0}\prec t^{-(k_{1}+1)}
by (
5.27
)-(
5.29
), and the perturbation argument as described in (
5.11
). Using (i)equation (
B.7
) with
d_{ij}
replaced by
0
, and (ii)the following rank inequality,

\displaystyle|h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})-h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0,\chi_{ij}=0}\leq 2\eta_{0}\big(\|G^{\gamma,d_{ij}}_{(ij)}(z_{t})\|+\|G^{\gamma,0}_{(ij)}(z_{t})\|\big)\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0,\chi_{ij}=0}\leq 2,

(B.10)

with the fact that
h_{\gamma,(ij)}(d_{ij},\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0,\chi_{ij}=0}\prec 1
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0,\chi_{ij}=0}\prec t^{-2k_{2}}.

(B.11)

Combining the above estimates and choosing
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{b}/8}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle|(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}k_{2}}|\lesssim\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N^{2+2\epsilon_{a}+(k_{1}+k_{2}-3)\epsilon_{b}}t^{k_{1}+1+2k_{2}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2+2\epsilon_{a}}}.

Case 3:

k_{1}+k_{2}=3
. The estimation in this case is similar to Case 2 above, but we need to use the bound
g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\prec 1
when
i\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
and
j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}
. Recall that
|\mathcal{D}_{r}|\vee|\mathcal{D}_{c}|\leq N^{1-\epsilon_{d}}
. Then we have

\displaystyle|(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}k_{2}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N^{2+2\epsilon_{a}}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}}+\frac{1}{N^{2-\epsilon_{d}}}\cdot\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N^{2\epsilon_{a}+\epsilon_{d}}t^{k_{1}+1+2k_{2}}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot(1-\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2+\epsilon_{a}}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}}+\frac{1}{N^{2-\epsilon_{d}+\epsilon_{a}}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\cdot(1-\mathbf{1}_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{r},j\in\mathcal{T}_{c}}),

where in the last step, we used the fact
t\gg N^{-\epsilon_{d}/8}
.

Case 4:

k_{1}+k_{2}=1
. In this case, using (
B.9
) we may compute that

\displaystyle(I_{1})_{ij,k_{1}k_{2}}={\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big[\gamma a_{ij}^{2}\big]\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,0)\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,0)\big)\bigg|\chi_{ij}=0\bigg]\cdot\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=0)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

We note that there will be corresponding terms in
(I_{2})_{ij}
, and these terms will cancel out with the ones described above.

Combining the estimates in the above cases, we can obtain that there exists some constant
\delta_{1}=\delta_{1}(\epsilon_{a})
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}(I_{1})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0,\\
k_{1}+k_{2}=1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big[\gamma a_{ij}^{2}\big]\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,0)\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,0)\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=0)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\delta_{1}}).

(B.12)

Next, we consider the estimation for
(I_{2})_{ij}
. When
\psi_{ij}=1
, we can apply Gaussian integration by parts to obtain that

\displaystyle|(I_{2})_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}|\lesssim\frac{t^{1/2}}{N}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\Big|\partial_{w_{ij}}\big\{g_{(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})F^{\prime}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(e_{ij},c_{ij})\big)\big\}\Big|\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}\lesssim\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{Nt}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1},

where the last step follows from (
5.27
)-(
5.29
).
The estimation for
(I_{2})_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}
is similar to those of
(I_{1})_{ij}
, we omit repetitive details. In summary, with the independence between
z_{t}
and
w_{ij}
, we have by possibly adjusting
\delta_{1}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}(I_{2})_{ij}=\sum_{i,j}(I_{2})_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+\sum_{i,j}(I_{2})_{ij}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0,\\
k_{1}+k_{2}=1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big[\gamma tw_{ij}^{2}\big]{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\delta_{1}}).

(B.13)

Note by (
5.18
) and the choices of
\epsilon_{a}
and
\epsilon_{b}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big[\gamma a_{ij}^{2}\big]\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=0)-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\big[\gamma tw_{ij}^{2}\big]=\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{t}{N^{2+2\epsilon_{b}}}\bigg).

This together with the
t
dependent bounds for
g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}
and
\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}/(\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}})
implies that it suffices to bound the following quantity:

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}:=\bigg({\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1},0)}(0,0)\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,0)\big)\bigg]\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

To provide a more precise distinction between (
B.12
) and (
B.13
), we let

\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(z^{(ij)}_{t}(\beta)):=g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1})}(0,\beta)\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}F^{\prime}}{\partial d_{ij}^{k_{2}}}\big(h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,\beta)\big).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}=\bigg({\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(0)\big)\bigg]\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}.

We may apply Taylor expansion to obtain that

\displaystyle\bigg({\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(0)\big)\bigg]\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\chi_{ij}^{2}b_{ij}^{2}\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\frac{\partial^{2}\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}^{2}}(b)\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\chi_{ij}^{2}b_{ij}^{2}\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\bigg(\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}}(b)\bigg)^{2}\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0},

(B.14)

with
b\in[0,B_{ij}]
. Here the first oder term disappeared due to symmetry.
To bound the above terms we need to first verify that
z_{t}(b)
still lies inside
\mathsf{D}
(w.h.p). This can be done by noting that for the replacement matrix
X_{(ij)}(b)
which replace the
B_{ij}
by
b
in
X
still satisfies the
\eta^{*}
- regularity. Therefore by Weyl’s inequality,

\displaystyle|\lambda_{-,t}(\chi_{ij}b_{ij})-\lambda_{-,t}(b)|

\displaystyle\prec|\lambda_{-,t}(\chi_{ij}b_{ij})-\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))|+|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X))-\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X_{(ij)}(b)))|

\displaystyle+|\lambda_{M}(\mathcal{S}(X_{(ij)}(b)))-\lambda_{-,t}(b)|\prec N^{-2/3}+N^{-\epsilon_{b}}+N^{-2/3}\prec N^{-\epsilon_{b}}.

(B.15)

Applying the perturbation argument as in (
5.11
) to relate
g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1})}(0,b)
back to
g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1})}(d_{ij},b)
, and then using (
B.15
) to verify that
z^{(ij)}_{t}(b)\in\mathsf{D}
, we can see that the bound
g_{(ij)}^{(k_{1})}(0,b)\prec t^{-(k_{1}+1)}
still holds. Similarly, we can also obtain
h^{(k_{2})}_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,b)\prec t^{-k_{2}}
for
k_{2}\geq 1
. For the case when
k_{2}=0
, we may use (
B.10
) and the fact that
N\eta_{0}\mathrm{Im}\,m^{\gamma}(z^{(ij)}_{t}(b))\prec 1
to conclude that
h_{\gamma,(ij)}(0,b)\prec 1
. Combining the above bounds with a Cauchy integral argument, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\prec\frac{1}{\eta_{0}t^{2}},\quad\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\prec\frac{1}{\eta_{0}^{2}t^{2}}.

Further using Lemma
5.4
, we have for arbitrary (small)
\epsilon>0
and (large)
D>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Omega:=\Big\{\Big|\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\frac{\partial^{2}\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}^{2}}(b)\Big|\leq\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N\eta_{0}t^{7}}\Big\}\bigcap\Big\{\Big|\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\bigg(\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}}(b)\bigg)^{2}\Big|\leq\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N^{2}\eta_{0}^{2}t^{8}}\Big\}\bigg)\geq 1-N^{-D}.

Since

\displaystyle\chi_{ij}^{2}b_{ij}^{2}\Big(\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\frac{\partial^{2}\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}^{2}}(b)+\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\bigg(\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}}(b)\bigg)^{2}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)-\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(0)\big)\Big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}-\Big(\chi_{ij}b_{ij}F^{\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}(z_{t}(0))\cdot\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}}(0)\Big)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0},

the deterministic upper bound for the left hand side of the above equation follows from (
5.20
) in Lemma
5.4
and the fact that
\mathrm{Im}\,z_{t}\geq N^{-1}
. Then we may follow the steps as in (
B.6
) to obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\bigg[\chi_{ij}^{2}b_{ij}^{2}\Big(\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\frac{\partial^{2}\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}^{2}}(b)+\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(b)\big)\cdot\bigg(\frac{\partial\lambda_{-,t}}{\partial B_{ij}}(b)\bigg)^{2}\Big)\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}\lesssim\frac{N^{\epsilon}}{N^{2}\eta_{0}t^{7}}

Therefore, with the fact that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[\gamma a_{ij}^{2}]\mathbb{P}(\chi_{ij}=0)\sim t{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}[\gamma w_{ij}^{2}]=\gamma t/N
, we have by possibly adjusting
\delta_{1}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i,j}(I_{1})_{ij}-(I_{2})_{ij}\Big|=\sum_{i,j}\frac{\gamma t}{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0,\\
k_{1}+k_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\Big|{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(\chi_{ij}b_{ij})\big)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\Psi}\Big[\mathsf{F}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big(z_{t}(0)\big)\Big]\Big|\mathbf{1}_{\psi_{ij}=0}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\delta_{1}})=\mathcal{O}(N^{-\delta_{1}}).

This together with the arguments as in (
5.46
)-(
5.47
) completes the proof of (
B.5
).
The proof for the case
\alpha=8/3
closely parallels, and is in fact simpler, primarily due to the absence of randomness in
\lambda_{\mathsf{shift}}
. Thus we omit the details. This concludes the proof.
∎
